#5171. Hyperliquid Burns $1.53 Million in HYPE Tokens in 24 Hours Amid Price Dip

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Decentralized exchange Hyperliquid has bought back and burned 21,200 HYPE over the past 24 hours, spending $1.53 million on the purchase at an average price of $72.31, according to Onchain Lens.

The on-chain analytics platform also reported that Hyperliquid has permanently removed a total of 50.47 million HYPE from circulation since the launch of the token. That is 5.05% of the max supply of 1 billion HYPE, and at the current price, the burned tokens are worth around $3.51 billion.

The latest buyback comes amid the decline in the price of the cryptocurrency. On Nov. 12, the price of HYPE went down by 3.45%, and at press time, the token stands at $69.52, according to TradingView. Earlier this autumn, the price of HYPE was above $90.

Hyperliquid pays for the buybacks in dollars, so the decentralized exchange is able to buy more HYPE with the same amount of money when the price of the token goes down.

DeFiLlama data shows that Hyperliquid has earned $1.41 billion for HYPE holders since December 2024. Over the past 30 days, the decentralized exchange earned $68.82 million, and its earnings for the past week stand at $15.04 million.

Over the last day, the platform collected about $1.46 million in fees, slightly less than the amount it spent on the buyback. Besides, DeFiLlama data shows that Hyperliquid currently earns more in fees than Ethereum, Tron and Base.

The largest part of the burned tokens, 48.85 million HYPE, was bought back by the Assistance Fund. The fund automatically receives between 97% and 99% of the fees from perpetual and spot trading on Hyperliquid, and the rest of the fees go to the HLP liquidity pool and to the builders of individual markets.

The Assistance Fund buys HYPE on the open market and sends the tokens to the "0xfefe...fefe" address. It is a system address that does not have a private key. In December 2025, Hyperliquid validators formally recognized the tokens held at this address as burned.

In August 2026, Hyperliquid also introduced the AQAv2 upgrade. Under the upgrade, around 90% of the yield generated by the USDC reserves on the platform now goes to the Assistance Fund. Each of these transfers brings between $14 million and $16 million to the fund.

Besides the Assistance Fund, HYPE is also burned through network fees. Around 1.24 million HYPE has been burned through fees and slashing on HyperCore, the base layer of Hyperliquid. Another 376,060 HYPE has been burned as gas fees on HyperEVM, the smart contract layer of the network.

#5170. XRP proposed lending vaults lock deposits and expose holders to risk

Proposed XRPL lending rules would lock XRP vault withdrawals during investment periods, exposing depositors to borrower repayment risk.

Proposed V1.1 rules for the XRP Ledger (XRPL) would require new loan brokers to use closed-ended native-lending vaults, forgoing withdrawals during the investment period and leaving depositors with shares exposed to borrower repayments.

In a Nov. 12 snapshot, XRPLWin showed LendingProtocol and LendingProtocolV1_1 each at 22 of 35 trusted-validator votes, with SingleAssetVault at 23 of 35. The explorer displayed a 29-vote threshold, and XRPL’s amendment rules require more than 80% support maintained for two weeks.

Under Ripple’s proposed V1.1 transaction rules, newly created loan brokers would have to attach to closed-ended vaults. Brokers created before that amendment activates could continue originating loans against open-ended vaults.

During subscription, depositors can add assets and withdraw, while both actions are blocked and capital can fund loans during investment. Redemption reopens withdrawals, while new deposits and new loans are barred.

The lending mechanism transfers requested principal to the borrower, excluding fees. Once XRP is lent, the depositor holds a claim through vault shares while the borrower controls the disbursed tokens.

A deposit made with already-owned XRP does not establish a market purchase, and an investment-period withdrawal lock also does not permanently immobilize the XRP passed to borrowers.

The amount held idle in vaults, the amount outstanding on loan, and what borrowers do with it would have to be measured separately before drawing conclusions about tradable supply.

XRPL’s design uses fixed-term, uncollateralized loans and off-chain underwriting, so holders take on exposure to borrowers’ ability to repay and the broker’s credit decisions.

In an XRP lending vault, borrower-paid XRP interest could increase the XRP claim represented by those shares, while losses could reduce it. That keeps the investment denominated in XRP without guaranteeing the number of tokens ultimately recovered.

A broker may deposit the same asset in a separate account to buffer defaults, but that protection is optional and configurable. Its presence does not guarantee repayment or eliminate depositor losses.

V1.1 would also put newly created vaults on cash-basis accounting, with interest becoming income when paid. Vaults created under the earlier whole-life model, which books scheduled interest at loan origination, would retain that accounting permanently.

The vault must permit transfers, and a recipient must be eligible to hold both the shares and the underlying asset. Private-vault credentials and applicable asset restrictions can prevent a transfer.

Even where ownership can move, a sale requires a willing buyer and an executable price.

Arf’s Oct. 22 announcement with Ripple noted that its first phase would provide direct RLUSD working capital on XRPL, with a planned move to native lending infrastructure after amendment activation. Underwriting would remain off-chain, and RLUSD would be the asset disbursed and repaid.

For holders, the next tests are amendment activation, funded XRP vaults, borrower drawdowns, paid interest, defaults and repayments.

Successful share sales would separately show whether an early exit is usable. Until those outcomes are observable, the proposed bargain for a closed-ended XRP vault is a withdrawal commitment in exchange for credit exposure, with borrower performance determining the income.

#5169. MSCI Drops Saylor's Strategy From Its Index Amid BTC Sell-Off

MSCI has published the results of its November 2026 Index Review, with Strategy among the companies set to leave the MSCI USA Index.

Metaplanet, the Tokyo-listed Bitcoin treasury company, is also scheduled for removal from the MSCI Japan Small Cap Index.

The changes will take effect after the market close on November 30, 2026.

Across the MSCI ACWI Index, 13 securities will be added and 100 deleted. The two additions to the MSCI World Index, measured by full company market capitalization, are Moderna (USA) and Aritzia (Canada).

The latest review follows the new rules proposed by MSCI in October, among which are new measures regarding crypto treasury holding companies.

MSCI evaluates whether a company maintains substantial operating assets and applies five financial ratio tests. In case a company fails to meet at least four criteria,

A company that fails at least four criteria across consecutive annual reporting periods may face removal from the MSCI Global Investable Market Indexes.

MSCI indexes track public companies based on market cap and investment risks. Global mutual funds and ETFs use these benchmarks to build their portfolios.

Dropping out of such index could influence investment flows as well as stock prices.

Strategy continues to accumulate BTC despite the crypto market staying in red zone.

Between November 2 and November 8, 2026, the company acquired another 1,036 BTC at an average price of $79,012.

As of November 12, Strategy holds 850,364 BTC. The company also reported about $5.5 billion in USD reserves.

However, Bitcoin's recent decline below $75,000 pushes Strategy's holdings into an unrealized loss of $500 million.

Strategy's stock (MSTR) fell 7% on Thursday, while Bitcoin is down 3.5% at the time of writing.

#5168. Everyone’s selling XRP right now, but why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP [XRP] has been sliding on the charts for weeks, and it looks like everyone’s selling their stash right now.

Whales have been steady though. They’ve been trading big, but we don’t quite know whose side they’re on.

Cryptoquant’s Futures Taker CVD has been sell-dominant since late August. That hasn’t changed yet so, two and a half months straight.

The only time this year Futures traders leaned buy-heavy was in the beginning of 2026. In that time, XRP has gone from $1.55 in late September to $1.05 now.

Spot traders used to turn a blind eye, but not anymore. For the most part, their CVD was neutral this year. There were some buy-heavy stretches in January, February and May.

That changed in October. Now, the Spot Take CVD is also sell-dominant. This is the first time that’s happened all year!

That matters more than you think. Futures selling might just be traders betting short. When Spot starts selling too, that would mean that people holding actual XRP may be getting out.

Usually, when people trade a coin this much, it’s because the price is rising. With XRP though, that’s not been the case.

Futures volume bubble map went red in late August. Through most of September and early October, it was marked as “overheating.” While that’s slowed down a bit since, it’s still been a fair spike.

It moved into “heating” in early September, was “overheating” in the middle of the month, and has stayed hot ever since.

Now, put that next to when Spot volume was “cooling” at $1.40.

Both markets are busy right now, but since it’s all during a dip, it may be indicative of selling.

Finally, CryptoQuant’s Average Order Size has shown “big whale orders” on Futures and Spot since early October.

That’s happened before as well. Whale-sized orders were all the rage through June, July, and most of August. All this, while XRP went between $1 and $1.25.

Towards the end of August, the price went back to $1.50 on the charts.

As with many others, this metric also only shows how big orders are, and not which way they’re going. Whales could be buying the dip, or walking away.

Either way, they’re the ones moving the most money right now. Whatever they decide at $1 is probably where XRP goes next.

Samyukhtha L KM is a financial journalist and market analyst at AMBCrypto. She covers key market moves, blockchain adoption, and socially-driven crypto trends. She also enjoys providing fresh takes through commentaries on emerging narratives.

#5167. Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Payward, the parent company of crypto exchange Kraken, has tokenized more than 200 UK and European stocks through its xStocks platform, it announced on Wednesday.

The first batch covers some of the most actively traded companies listed in the UK and the European Union, with more to be added on a rolling basis. Like other xStocks, each token is backed 1:1 by the underlying share, which is bought and held by a regulated custodian for as long as the token trades, according to Payward.

Payward's @xStocksFi is expanding beyond US tokenized equities.

In partnership with @GTNglobal, more than 200 of the UK and EU’s largest equities are now tokenized by xStocks, including HSBC, Shell, LVMH and Airbus.

The first international launch, with more markets to follow.…

The tokens are available to trade on Kraken and through other platforms in the xStocks Alliance, and on blockchains where xStocks already trade, including Solana, Ethereum and TON. They are offered in more than 110 markets, but remain unavailable to investors in the UK.

"A portfolio should not have borders or trading hours," said Mark Greenberg, Payward's chief commercial officer. "Tokenizing every other international market is the next step."

The launch takes xStocks past 1,500 tokenized stocks, ETFs and IPOs. The platform holds more than $1.1 billion in assets, has processed over $45 billion in total transaction volume, and has more than 550,000 holders, Payward said.

The new tokens are the first delivered under Payward's partnership with GTN, a brokerage infrastructure firm that handles execution and custody of the underlying shares. When the two partnered in July, Payward said it planned to start with Hong Kong-listed stocks; hundreds of Hong Kong listings will now follow in the coming weeks, the company said.

The expansion also delivers on part of Payward's partnership with the London Stock Exchange, announced in September, under which Payward agreed to tokenize the 100 largest London-listed companies. Subject to regulatory approval, the exchange intends to list xStocks and begin trading them on LSE 24, its planned near-24-hour trading venue, in 2027.

Payward has been drawing interest from traditional exchanges. Nasdaq agreed to invest $100 million in the company at a $21 billion valuation in September, building on a March partnership to bring tokenized stocks to market in 2027, while Deutsche Börse bought a $200 million stake in April.

The company has also been expanding through acquisitions, agreeing to buy derivatives exchange Bitnomial for $550 million and Lithuania's Saldo Bank as part of a push into the EU.

#5166. XRP Funding Rate Hits One-Year High, ETFs Still Bleed

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Funding Rate Hits One-Year High, ETFs Still Bleed

XRP’s hurdles on the ETF markets conflict with the generally-bullish sentiment on Perpetuals. Who’s right?

XRP’s Derivatives crypto traders have been on the look-out for a bear trap. The latest Binance Funding Rate data showcases a scenario where the 30-day cumulative rate reached 0.18%, the biggest point in more than a year.

CryptoQuant’s analyst Dark Frost has dug into the historical data behind the shift, witnessing a similar structure just two months before XRP hit $3.55 back in 2025. The rolling monthly figure is particularly important to swing traders looking for the price bottom, below all 4 moving averages.

Traders have taken a keen interest in the $0.98 – $1 liquidity pocket, the same that served XRP as a trampoline to $1.67 back in August, 2026. With the positioning on Binance’s Derivatives now on the bullish side, the sentiment needs confirmation from whales buying on Spot.

In regards to the XRP coin funding rate hike on Binance, that kind of shift often precedes a price trend reversal. “Extreme readings in either direction can signal that traders are crowding into the same side of the market”, says Dark Frost on X. The 30-day sum hints at a few scenarios.

🗞️ XRP funding rates on Binance hit a 1Y high while spot ETFs see $10.4M in net outflowsFunding rates for ripple:native perpetual futures on Binance have reached their highest level in more than a year. The last time rates were this high was in October 2025, a few months after… pic.twitter.com/ZZte6Vfud4

Over the period shown, peaks in bullish sentiment coincided with XRP price tops, while the most negative funding readings occurred near market bottoms. XRP has already entered a 30% price correction from the cycle peak, similarly to when XRP rebounded 53% after falling to $0.70.

For CryptoQuant’s experts, this resembles strong conviction. “XRP’s 30% correction illustrates the risk of maintaining a bullish bias as prices fall” said the analyst. However, that comes with a price on its own. The data from Futures markets tells a story of XRP bulls getting beat down.

The liquidation data has been lob-sided, with XRP’s bulls incurring a $11.01 million deficit in the 24-hour time-frame, says CoinGlass. The ratios from all five top-traded XRP coin venues are displaying long versus short figures drastically below 1, meaning a bearish crowd sentiment.

On Wall Street, the bearish sentiment is felt even harder. Spot XRP ETFs recorded one of their worst sessions of the year, with daily net outflows hitting 9.37 million XRP, equal to $10.4 million. The altcoin’s ETF items slimmed by $18 million in two days, leaving bears hungry for more.

#5165. XRP, SHIB, BTC and Other Tokens Hit Hard by Abrupt Resignation of Nvidia CEO

Top digital assets like Bitcoin (BTC), XRP, Ethereum (ETH) and Shiba Inu (SHIB) are facing heavy selling pressure following a severe setback in the crypto market after the sudden exit of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang.

The unexpected exit of the Nvidia co-founder has created pandemonium in the financial world as shares of the artificial intelligence company have been hit by a heavy sell-off, further straining an already weak crypto market.

According to reports by Reuters, Nvidia chief executive Jensen Huang resigned on Nov. 12. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress has taken over as interim chief executive.

There are controversies surrounding Huang's personal life and doubts about his future at the company he co-founded over three decades ago.

The news of Huang's exit has triggered a heavy sell-off in Nvidia stock, with shares dropping over 12% in early trading, wiping out an estimated $500 billion in market value.

The sell-off has also affected crypto-related trading markets.

As recently revealed by blockchain analytics platform Lookonchain, a trader known as 0x7368 had his leveraged long position in Nvidia stock fully liquidated.

According to Lookonchain, the trader had 9,959 units of Nvidia (NVDA), worth about $1.54 million, liquidated after news of Jensen Huang's resignation hit the market.

Meanwhile, the crypto market itself is reeling from heavy losses as some top altcoins are faring worse than Bitcoin.

The recent crypto market data shows that Bitcoin (BTC) is currently trading around $71,323, shedding about 3% in the last 24 hours.

Over the last seven days, the largest cryptocurrency by market cap has lost even more, shedding about 10.2%.

However, XRP has seen a more pronounced sell-off.

The Ripple-linked cryptocurrency currently trades at about $1.02 after losing 3.3% in the last 24 hours.

#5164. Here’s why Zcash could remove 50,000 ZEC from its grants treasury

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Zcash [ZEC] community is gearing up to vote on a proposal that could significantly change how ZEC funds its development and distributes newly created tokens. The plan involves removing 50,000 ZEC from the Zcash Community Grants (ZCG) treasury and redirecting its value toward future mining rewards.

That said, Zcash currently allocates 8% of newly issued ZEC to ZCG, which uses the funds to support developers, wallet providers, and other ecosystem projects.

These grants help maintain the infrastructure and software needed to use ZEC. However, rising ZEC prices has increased the value of ZCG’s existing treasury, which has raised concerns that the committee was accumulating more funds than necessary while continuing to receive new token allocations.

However, now, under the new proposal, approximately 55% of ZCG’s treasury (50,000 ZEC) would be removed from circulation within 30 days of activation. Going ahead, its value would be credited to the Network Sustainability Mechanism, introduced through the NU7 upgrade. This, in turn, would gradually return ZEC to the network through future mining rewards.

At the same time, the proposal would also end ZCG’s existing 8% allocation of newly created ZEC. Instead, this share would accumulate in a new Future Dev and Legal Defense Fund until the next halving takes place.

As this happens, the fund would remain inaccessible until a separate proposal establishes withdrawal rules. Lastly, ZCG would retain its remaining treasury to continue funding development, while the separate 12% Coinholder-Controlled Fund would remain unchanged.

For those unaware, the revised plan follows ZCG’s 3rd November decision to abandon an earlier proposal, which faced criticism for seeking to redirect approximately 98,000 ZEC from existing assets and future income toward mining rewards.

So, now, the next step is community voting, which is scheduled from 16th November to 30th November. Coinholders and community panels will assess the proposal’s support.

If it receives rough consensus, developers, and miners can work toward implementing it through a future network upgrade called NU7.1. However, there is no confirmed activation date yet.

This coincided with Zcash’s monthly active developer count having nearly doubled over the past two years.

According to Glassnode’s report, around 70% of its active developers have more than one year of experience in crypto development, compared with 58% across the wider industry.

Still, ZEC’s price action was under bearish pressure as it was trading at $986.40 at press time after a drop of 1.4% in the past 24 hours.

This followed a recent report by AMBCrypto wherein ZEC fell by 15.25% and Open Interest also declined 13.4%, indicating weakening speculative demand.

#5163. Bitcoin and Ethereum sink to 3-month lows, but a bigger crypto selloff may be ahead

A $727 million liquidation wave has exposed the market's fragility, with Bitcoin facing a critical $68,000 support test and deeper historical downside risks.

Bitcoin and Ethereum fell to three-month lows Thursday, deepening a cryptocurrency selloff that has erased $550 billion in market value since October.

Bitcoin slumped to $71,317, its lowest level since August, while Ethereum briefly fell below $2,000 to touch $1,983. The declines have pushed the broader crypto market's valuation from approximately $2.94 trillion in early October to $2.39 trillion.

The downturn has coincided with a sharp deterioration in market sentiment. CryptoQuant's Analyst Consensus Index fell from -1.0 to -1.8 standard deviations over seven days, signaling increasingly bearish expectations as investors grapple with persistent selling pressure and uncertainty over the market's next direction.

The latest price decline triggered widespread losses across cryptocurrency derivatives markets as leveraged traders were caught on the wrong side of the downturn.

According to CoinGlass, 125,731 traders were liquidated over 24 hours, with closed positions totaling about $727 million.

Bitcoin accounted for $293.8 million of those liquidations, with bullish positions representing 91.1% of the total. Ethereum followed with $120.2 million, of which 87.7% came from traders betting on higher prices.

Together, Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for approximately $414 million, or 57% of the market-wide total.

Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for about $414 million of the roughly $727 million market total, according to a rolling 24-hour CoinGlass snapshot on Nov. 12.

The selling intensified during a four-hour period when nearly $489 million in leveraged positions were liquidated. Long positions accounted for approximately $446 million of those losses, compared with $44 million in short positions.

The largest individual liquidation was a $31 million Bitcoin position on decentralized derivatives exchange Hyperliquid.

The concentration of losses among bullish traders illustrates how the price decline forced exchanges to close leveraged positions, adding to selling pressure as the market struggled to find support.

The downturn comes during weakening institutional demand and contracting liquidity across the crypto market.

Data from SoSoValue showed that US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds have recorded approximately $2 billion in net outflows over the past three weeks. Ethereum-focused products are also heading toward a seventh consecutive week of withdrawals, with more than $2.1 billion leaving the funds during that period.

The sustained withdrawals have coincided with a broader contraction in available crypto liquidity.

The supply of major dollar-linked stablecoins contracted by $14.2 billion over 30 days, while Bitcoin's apparent demand declined by 284,000 BTC.

These developments suggest that the latest liquidation wave struck a market already struggling to attract fresh capital, leaving fewer sources of demand to absorb the selling pressure.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Beyond the immediate selling pressure, Bitcoin's deteriorating technical structure and historical valuation indicators point to the market could face further losses before establishing a durable bottom.

The cryptocurrency has fallen below its 200-day moving average, which stood near $73,200, breaking a widely followed trend indicator used to assess the broader direction of prices.

The breakdown has shifted attention toward lower price levels where traders expect buying interest to emerge.

According to CoinGlass, Bitcoin's liquidation heatmap identifies approximately $68,000 as the next potential support region. A decline toward that level would extend the current downturn and test whether buyers can absorb additional selling pressure.

However, historical on-chain valuation data shows that Bitcoin could face a considerably deeper correction if market conditions continue to deteriorate.

Joao Wedson, chief executive of blockchain analytics firm Alphractal, highlighted Bitcoin's Cumulative Value Days Destroyed (CVDD) indicator, which currently places a historical bear-market valuation reference near $52,700.

The metric uses the value and age of Bitcoin moving on-chain to identify price regions associated with previous market bottoms.

At a Bitcoin price of $72,500, the cryptocurrency was trading approximately 37.7% above the CVDD level. A complete retest would imply a further decline of roughly 27% from that reference price.

The $72,500 comparison price puts CoinGlass's estimated $68,000 liquidation heatmap region about 6.2% lower and Joao Wedson's $52,700 CVDD reading about 27.3% lower.

Source: CoinGlass liquidation heatmap · Alphractal Academy (CVDD methodology)

Wedson pointed to previous market cycles in which Bitcoin approached or briefly traded below the indicator.

In December 2018, Bitcoin bottomed near $3,122 against a CVDD reading of $3,060. Similarly, the November 2022 market bottom occurred around $15,479, slightly below the indicator's $15,900 valuation.

Comparable patterns emerged during the January 2015 and March 2020 downturns, when Bitcoin traded below the metric before eventually recovering.

Still, the historical relationship does not establish that Bitcoin must revisit $52,700 during the current downturn.

Unlike a fixed technical support level, CVDD changes with on-chain activity as older Bitcoin moves between wallets. Its valuation threshold can rise over time, potentially narrowing the gap between the indicator and market prices without requiring an equivalent decline in Bitcoin.

That leaves the market facing two distinct price tests: whether buyers can defend the $68,000 region identified by CoinGlass, and whether a sustained recovery can push Bitcoin back above its 200-day moving average.

A failure to stabilize around those levels would strengthen concerns that the latest selloff is developing into a more prolonged correction, bringing the deeper historical valuation levels highlighted by Wedson into focus.

#5162. 'Yes': SBI CEO Yoshitaka Kitao Answers XRP Holder Doubts in One Word

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Yoshitaka Kitao, head of Japanese financial group SBI Holdings, answered "YES" when asked point-blank whether he still trusts XRP.

The question came from an X user. The user wrote that the past few weeks had been "truly exhausting" for XRP holders, and said that token holders feel that Ripple has left them behind.

Kitao answered in English. Most of his recent posts on X are in Japanese, and the rest of them are reposts of other accounts.

Kitao previously worked as an executive at Nomura Securities and later served as CFO of SoftBank. In 1999, he founded SoftBank Investment, which later became SBI Holdings. Today, SBI includes banks, brokerage, and insurance businesses, as well as crypto services.

The partnership began in 2016. SBI joined Ripple's Series B round and launched a joint venture with Ripple, SBI Ripple Asia. SBI owns about 60% of the venture, and Ripple owns the remaining 40%.

The same year, SBI Ripple Asia set up a consortium of Japanese banks to test Ripple's technology for payments, and the consortium later grew to more than 60 banks.

In 2018, SBI launched the interbank app MoneyTap. The same year, SBI's crypto exchange, now called SBI VC Trade, started operations with XRP trading. In 2019, Kitao joined Ripple's board of directors.

From 2020, SBI began handing the token out to shareholders as bonuses, and the payment service SBI Remit launched remittances using it with on-demand liquidity (ODL), including in the major Japan-Philippines corridor.

SBI kept working with Ripple during the SEC lawsuit against the company, which lasted from December 2020 until 2025.

Back on February 15, 2026, in rare long-form tweets in English, Kitao denied rumors that SBI holds $10 billion worth of XRP: "Not $10 billion in XRP, but around 9% of Ripple Labs. So our hidden asset could be much bigger." By his account, the combined valuation of Ripple Labs and its ecosystem is "enormous," and SBI owns more than 9% of all of it.

Kitao also talked about XRP at SBI's quarterly briefing on November 2, 2026. He said that investors buy Bitcoin mostly to hold it, and that the value of XRP depends on its use in payments and business ecosystems.

In August 2025, SBI VC Trade and Ripple agreed to bring the RLUSD stablecoin to Japan. In October 2025, SBI also invested $200 million in Evernorth, a company that holds XRP in its treasury. By now, SBI VC Trade has more than two million accounts.

#5161. Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💵 What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Several key new details for Anthropic’s pending IPO were released on Tuesday. The IPO price range was set at $70 to $78 a share, selling 825 million Class A shares to raise about $64.4 billion (at the top of the range). Pricing is expected November 18, with trading starting November 19 on Nasdaq under ANT. Roughly 20% of the deal is set aside for retail, which is about double what a book this size usually gives individual investors.

Notably on Hyperliquid, ANT is trading at $1.8T with $100M in daily trading volume and $200M in open interest (a big enough market to provide some signal). So perhaps traders are a bit more bullish on ANT price action than Wall Street.

Alongside the IPO sits a $26 billion private placement of non-voting Class N stock, bought at the IPO price by seven of Anthropic’s own compute suppliers. Google and Nvidia take about $10 billion each, AMD $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion apiece, TSMC $500 million. Those shares carry no lock-up. Combined with the IPO, Anthropic walks away with roughly $84.6 billion for general corporate purposes, mostly compute.

Regarding financials, Anthropic just reported revenue of $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier and a $1.26 billion operating loss. It reported a $104 billion net loss, almost all of it a non-cash charge on convertible notes. And $900.9 billion in committed spending, with $70.7 billion due inside twelve months.

The liquidity drain thesis has been circulating since April, when CoinDesk laid out the case that mega-IPOs pull speculative money from the same pool that funds crypto. Retail allocations are the mechanism. A dollar earmarked for an IPO ticket is a dollar not in Bitcoin or memecoins.

We already ran this experiment. SpaceX listed June 12 at more than $2 trillion. Bitcoin fell 29% going into it, bottomed at $58,500 on June 30, and then rose 37% off that low. But notably, the Bitcoin bottom was effectively in on June 5 at $59k, a full week ahead of the SPCX IPO.

So the question is not whether an IPO pulls money out. It’s whether that already happened. Bitcoin fell roughly 15% from its early-October high through a $6.3 billion exchange hack, an AI capex selloff and a Democratic sweep.

Two things make this deal smaller than it looks for crypto. The retail slice is roughly $12.9 billion against SpaceX’s $22 billion, about 59% as much. And the $26 billion private placement never touches public market liquidity at all, because it comes from Anthropic’s vendors, who then get paid back in compute contracts.

Thus, if the SpaceX pattern repeats, and with the ~60% factor, we could expect BTC to drop another 5% or so into the Anthropic IPO (notably, that’s right where the 200-week moving average is). And we would expect the effective bottom to be in sometime in the next 1-2 days.

The good news is, the pain is mostly behind us, in that scenario. We will find out soon enough if IPO history repeats itself for crypto…

#5160. Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Platforms are able to conduct business operations in crypto, offering retail knowledge-based accessibility.

Russia’s crypto bid is going at a faster pace since the fourth largest economy around the globe introduced crypto trading for retail & businesses. The new set of rules, coming from the Central Bank Of Russia, limits regular investors to a 300,000 rubles yearly cap (roughly $3,600).

This allows buying & holding Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH) & USDT through regulated intermediaries (brokers, trust managers & licensed exchanges). Everyone must pass a knowledge test plus review risk disclosures before starting trading activities, while XRP is considered an asset for advanced users only.

The Moscow Exchange already lists cash-settled perpetual futures (XRP/USDF) and related indices tracking XRP prices. These are available only to qualified investors, settle in rubles (no actual XRP is delivered), and form part of a broader set of crypto derivatives with substantial turnover among Futures traders across the scene.

Russian Central Bank: Exchanges May Conduct Crypto Trading from November 22, Subject to Prior Approval of Trading Rules According to TASS, the Bank of Russia said Russian exchanges may begin offering cryptocurrency trading from November 22, 2026, provided they first agree on… pic.twitter.com/txoYyq5vfL

The fresh legal framework dates back to August. Then, Russia’s President Vladimir Putin signed the inaugural comprehensive cryptocurrency law (Federal Law No. 282-FZ, “On Digital Currencies and Digital Rights”), setting September 1, 2026 activation window: contrastingly to the entry-level investor restrictions, qualified ones have no limits.

Meanwhile, businesses face hard limits, needing to meet a minimum equity requirement (15 million rubles, roughly $187,000) to run crypto services or custody services. The regulatory framework is way more lenient on businesses conducting cross-border sanctions to dodge the 21-chapter European Union sanction packages.

The retail crypto trading gets legalized on November 22, 2026, the related Duma documents state. The approved exchanges & crypto brokers are obliged to report the trading data and results to the central bank in accordance with existing reporting procedures for XRP & others.

For XRP traders in the region, the MOEX XRP Futures index allows up to 5x leverage.

RUSSIA MADE A MASSIVE $XRP MOVE!! pic.twitter.com/dmuUaPnsuP

Paying with XRP or any other popular crypto coin at a local store is still off the table. Goods & services are prohibited as a result of a separate ban, pushing crypto adoption in the perspective of an investment vehicle rather than a means of payment.

Still, setting up state-surveilled crypto brokers is a pretty big move that could inspire other major economies to do the same.

#5159. How Ethereum staking hit a record 36.3% despite ETH’s price drop

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum’s [ETH] staking ratio has reportedly reached an all-time high (ATH)  of 36.3%, accounting for more than one-third of ETH’s total circulating supply. Well, the rising staking ratio suggests that an increasing number of ETH holders are choosing to lock up their tokens rather than sell or actively trade them.

This comes as Ethereum’s validator queues show that at press time, 911,938 ETH was waiting to be staked with a 16-day, 7-hour wait. Meanwhile, 264,211 ETH was queued to exit with a 5-day, 16-hour wait, plus a 7-day sweep delay.

Both queues are limited by the same churn rate of 256 per epoch, meaning only a fixed amount of ETH can join or leave each epoch, which is why a big backlog translates into longer waits.

In other words, more people want to stake than unstake right now, but the entry line is slowly shrinking, and exiting takes roughly 12-13 days in total once the sweep delay is included.

This comes as the total ETH staked rose from about 42.1 million ETH (roughly 34.5% of supply) in mid-August to about 44.5 million ETH (around 36.5%) at press time. Since late October the climb has resumed steadily.

Of this, BitMine earned $121.4 million from staking and validation activities, accounting for approximately 97% of its total revenue. By the 8th of November, the largest Ethereum DAT held 6.07 million ETH, of which 5.22 million ETH was staked.

That said, the company also estimates that these holdings could generate around $309 million in annual staking rewards, based on an estimated 2.65% yield and an ETH price of $2,234.

On the other hand, SharpLink has accumulated 31,363 ETH in staking rewards as of 9th November. Its strategy also includes liquid staking through Lido, allowing it to receive wstETH in return for its deposited ETH.

All of this happened while the active validators moved in the opposite direction. As per charts, they held near 900K through mid-September, peaking around 912K. But again, they dropped sharply from mid-September to mid-October, falling to about 815K, and have since drifted down to roughly 807K, with a small additional drop on the last day or two.

However, the price action of ETH trading at $2,042.94 at press time after a modest drop in the past 24 hours but a 15.5% drop in the past week sparks concern. This comes as Ethereum faces renewed selling pressure after losing its $2,250 support level. However, Vitalik Buterin’s recent push to position ETH as a store of value suggests hope.

#5157. Helius encrypts Solana transfers without removing operator trust

Its documented default prover, the service that constructs zero-knowledge proofs, adds a hardware requirement where secret transaction inputs are processed inside an attested AWS Nitro enclave, an isolated computing environment.

Solana can check that a transfer satisfies the protocol’s rules, but protecting the inputs while that proof is made depends on the prover’s isolation, code, and deployment policies.

The practical question for wallet developers integrating the launch is which of those protections their software can verify.

Helius’s wallet integration guide says proof generation needs plaintext inputs covering the complete transaction data and nullifier keys. It identifies an attested AWS Nitro enclave as the SDKs’ default prover.

The protocol specification lists private inputs including the asset, amount, blinding values, and nullifier secret for notes being spent, along with paths proving those notes belong to the state tree. The proof also includes output assets, amounts, and blinding values.

Those inputs become readable to the proving code inside the enclave, and the documented client protects them from the host operator by encrypting requests to a key held by the attested enclave.

The nullifier secret also derives the markers used to prevent repeat spending. In the native wallet integration, the wallet retains the signing key, and the TypeScript SDK leaves signing of the finished Solana transaction with the application’s signer.

Helius documents local decryption, which keeps the viewing key on the device, and delegated decryption, which shares a viewing key with an authorized provider. Delegation gives that provider read access to balances and history, without granting spending authority.

AWS describes Nitro Enclaves as isolated environments whose memory the parent instance’s processes or administrators cannot access. Enclaves have no persistent storage, interactive access, or external networking.

The tagged SDK refuses a remote prover request without one, before making an HTTP call. A wallet can pin a reviewed release policy or use a network policy authenticated by the SDK’s pinned signers. Signed policies refresh during attestation and expire after use.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Helius’s documented default path sends encrypted proof inputs into an AWS Nitro enclave, then returns the proof to the wallet before transaction signing.

The client verifies attestation against an approved policy before encrypting remote proof requests.

Code review, AWS isolation and the operator’s initial KMS policy binding underpin the documented confidentiality design.

Wallets check an approved enclave and key; code review and deployment policy remain separate dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

Qualitative data-flow diagram of the documented native-wallet default prover. No numerical scale or calculated values. The selectable panel distinguishes client attestation checks from code, hardware and deployment trust dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

A wallet can automatically check that the enclave matches an approved software image and encryption key, yet deciding which image to approve still requires confidence in the code and its reviewers.

The Nitro deployment guide says the measured image includes the prover, startup script, certificate bundle, allowed external hosts, indexer URL, and KMS key identity. Its design uses AWS Key Management Service to release the shared encryption seed only to matching attested enclaves.

The guide says clients cannot read the KMS key policy and rely on the operator’s deployment for that guarantee. It identifies AWS KMS, AWS Support, Nitro hardware, and the operator as trust roots during initial policy binding.

Even correctly encrypted requests leave metadata visible to the parent, such as paths, queries, timing, and sizes. The path reveals the proof’s circuit shape.

The versioned TEE guide records a mainnet build based on v0.6.0-alpha, with measured-build provenance, distinct from the ts-sdk-v0.8.0-alpha documentation tag.

The specification allows client-side proofs, and the repository supplies localnet tooling and downloadable prover binaries.

Helius’s launch announcement says Certora, zkSecurity, and OtterSec audited programs and circuits. Its security page lists Certora in October and zkSecurity in August, while marking OtterSec as awaiting a report.

The launch gives wallet developers a trust decision on which code and deployment policy they approve to handle proof inputs.

#5156. Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

What happens when the place you read the news becomes the place you act on it?

The news that moves markets and the tools to trade them have long lived in separate places. Decrypt is bringing them together.

Decrypt is integrating perpetual futures trading, powered by Phoenix, a fully onchain perpetuals exchange on Solana, making it the first media platform to offer perps trading directly alongside its journalism.

Readers will be able to take long or short positions on crypto, stocks and commodities from the same place they follow the news that moves them, without switching apps, connecting external wallets or moving funds between platforms.

"Information influences decisions, shapes opinions and moves markets," said Ilan Hazan, co-founder and COO of Decrypt. "A reader might discover an opportunity through a publication, develop a view and then leave to act on it somewhere else. The publisher monetizes the impression, while someone else monetizes the transaction. It means the publisher captures only a small part of the economic activity that its information and distribution generates."

Rather than treating media and financial products as separate businesses, he said, Decrypt is bringing them together. "We're allowing readers to go from understanding what's happening in the markets to taking a position, without leaving Decrypt.”

"Partnering with Decrypt closes the gap between reading the news and acting on it," said Kabir Sethi, Head of Business Development at Phoenix. "With over 100 Phoenix perpetuals markets built directly into Decrypt, readers can take in a story, form a view, and trade it in the same place. We are excited to help play a part in transforming Decrypt into a true information exchange."

Perpetual futures, or perps, are contracts that let traders bet on an asset's price rising or falling without an expiry date, typically using leverage. Leverage magnifies both gains and losses, and positions can be liquidated quickly when markets move against them.

Phoenix offers markets in crypto, U.S. stocks, gold and pre-IPO companies around the clock, with up to 40x leverage and low fees, according to its website. It is developed by Ellipsis Labs, a team that has built exclusively on Solana and whose CEO, Eugene Chen, has described perps as having "crazy product market fit."

"These are very, very strong, high revenue businesses, probably some of the best in the industry," Chen said of perps exchanges in a podcast interview with Blockworks' Lightspeed earlier this year. Phoenix routes each trade at the moment it lands onchain, splitting it between its order book and market makers "based on what's going to give you the best price," he explained.

Perps trading runs through the Decrypt Money Account, a single self-custodial account on Solana designed to work without seed phrases, gas fees or bridging.

One balance now funds it all: prediction markets on Decrypt Predict, Jupiter-powered token swaps, Earn and, now, perpetual futures. Users can also send funds privately, after Decrypt added confidential transfers through Solana infrastructure firm Helius.

Imagine reading about a public company's earnings and taking a position on its stock, at any hour. Or following a breaking story about Bitcoin and going long or short in the same moment.

From information to execution, in one place.

Announced in September, The Information Exchange is Decrypt's vision for the next generation of financial media: a single destination bringing together news, intelligence, market data, probabilities, direct financial participation—with perps forming the latest building block in that infrastructure.

News moves markets. Now readers can move with it.

Perpetual futures are leveraged products and carry a high risk of loss. Availability may vary by region.

#5155. Ripple's RLUSD Overtakes PayPal's PYUSD

Ripple's US dollar-backed stablecoin RLUSD has set a new record by surpassing PayPal USD (PYUSD) in market capitalization to cement its position as a top stablecoin globally.

Recent data from CoinGecko shows that RLUSD's market cap has grown to around $2.71 billion, enabling it to surpass its competitor, PayPal's stablecoin, with a market cap of around $2.69 billion.

This milestone was achieved less than two years after Ripple's formal introduction of RLUSD in December 2024, underscoring its rapid growth in the crypto market.

A prominent XRP Ledger validator, Vet, also commented on this development to underscore the increasing significance of RLUSD in the stablecoin industry.

"RLUSD is now a Top 5 USD backed stablecoin. On its way to be in Top 3," Vet commented.

Vet noted Ripple's long-standing contribution to building the XRP Ledger. He added that the increasing adoption of RLUSD could potentially lead to faster adoption of the blockchain by traditional financial institutions.

Vet stressed the increasing relevance of RLUSD's rising payment volume and increased use of the stablecoin on the XRP Ledger.

He also noted that RLUSD has opened the door to numerous integrations with banks and crypto exchanges.

The XRP Ledger validator further noted that these integrations could motivate financial institutions to not only run infrastructure to support the XRPL but also integrate other tokenized assets on it.

This new record for RLUSD comes on the back of several key institutional partnerships and exchange integrations for RLUSD that Ripple has been announcing recently.

On Oct. 28, Ripple announced a partnership with Coinbase to provide RLUSD for retail and institutional users across Coinbase Exchange, Coinbase Prime and its retail trading app.

As part of the partnership, Coinbase also announced support for RLUSD across different blockchain networks such as the XRP Ledger, Ethereum and Base.

On the other hand, Ripple's partnership with banking giant BNY, which is the custodian of reserves for RLUSD, is also deepening.

Besides this, Ripple has also managed to secure support from Standard Chartered, which launched institutional custody services for RLUSD on the XRP Ledger back in October.

#5154. Binance Halts Luna Classic Burns: What’s Going On?

Home » Altcoins » Binance Halts Luna Classic Burns: What’s Going On?

Terra Luna Classic’s community got used to the monthly burning ceremonies, but this time it’s postponed.

A few weeks after Binance’s $6.4 billion hack following an employee’s laptop breach, the largest digital asset exchange across the globe is resuming most of their regular services. However, the Terra Luna Classic (LUNC) community has missed the monthly LUNC burn, coming from 50% of fees garnered from Binance’s LUNC-related trading pairs.

Typically done on the first of the month, this time Binance’s LUNC burn didn’t arrive at all, chain records show. That’s the first break in a pattern running since March 2023, when Binance pledged to help restore the downtrodden Terra Luna Classic community’s hope in LUNC’s long-term restoration efforts via continuous token burns.

On-chain records show 50 batches of LUNC burns being carried out by Binance on an earlier note. The last one, which was on October 1, 2026, eliminated 335 million tokens, pushing the exchange’s cumulative Terra Luna Classic burn count towards 91 billion.

That’s roughly 19.6% of all LUNC burns to this date, a considerable contribution in the deflationary campaign.

Still no statement from @Binance regarding the monthly #LUNC burn.. Although we understand the massive hack, I feel as though a statement should at least be made to #LunaClassic investors. Is this a temporary pause or indefinite?.. pic.twitter.com/Od2yH1kFgn

Some LUNC community members are floating the main reason for the delay to be apparent changes in wallet security, but Binance confirmed no such action. Naturally, the internal wallet reshuffling can be a normal practice following the multi-billion dollar hack and the day-by-day withdrawal restoration efforts done with diligence.

Back in August, the community behind Terra Luna Classic revisited the proposal 12223 to raise the on-chain transaction tax to 1.5%. Of that, 1.2 percentage points go towards burning even more LUNC coins. Meanwhile, strong voices in the crypto community have been advocating for more exchanges beyond Binance to burn fixed-fee allocations.

KuCoin & OKX were among the ones named in negotiations. The game-tested altcoin still has a massively over-minted supply of 5.5 trillion tokens in circulation, a figure normally ascribed to meme coins.

$LUNC AND $USTC BURN UPDATE pic.twitter.com/hboLcOFNdN

However, the LUNC chain restoration efforts have torched nearly 500 billion out of the original pile, showcasing what years of strict coordination can do for an embattled community.

Aside from the 19% portion claimed by Binance, the on-chain transaction tax and direct LUNC community burns make up the rest of the historical total. Regardless, the biggest hurdle for a proper Terra Luna Classic (LUNC) restoration point remains the stagnant trading volume.

Since the hard fork in May 2022, the cumulative burn efforts crunched 6.7% of total supply so far.

Token burning is a considerable deflation measure for long-term holders to ensure the asset remains at relatively bearable market value.

#5153. Can TRX/ETH extend its 23% rally after Tron takes the lead with USDT?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

A key shift is taking shape in the market, and it is one traders should keep a close eye on.

On a macro level, the stablecoin market is currently at one of its lowest levels since the 2022 bear market. The total supply of Tether (USDT) has fallen by almost $15 billion from its peak of $310 billion in mid-May. This indicated that liquidity has been flowing out of the crypto market, which is why demand has been pretty low even though Bitcoin is now trading almost 30% off its $126K high.

As the largest stablecoin hub, Ethereum has taken a significant hit too.

According to DeFiLlama, the total amount of stablecoins on Ethereum fell by over $20 billion. This was compounded by Tether as in the last 30 days alone, the total supply of USDT on Ethereum fell by nearly 9% to just over $66 billion. The key takeaway, however, may be Tron’s USDT supply.

As shown in the chart above, USDT supply on the Tron blockchain was down by only just over 1% at press time. This was much lower than the 9% decrease seen on Ethereum. This hinted at a clear divergence in stablecoin liquidity between the two networks.

With Ethereum seeing heavier USDT outflows, Tron’s share of total USDT supply climbed above 52%, reinforcing its position as the dominant network for USDT liquidity.

Interestingly, Tron founder Justin Sun also pointed out this trend in a recent X post, highlighting data from Token Terminal. In the past 12 months, TRON recorded a $16.1 billion hike in stablecoin supply, the highest among 45 tracked blockchains. HyperEVM came second. Arc also had an increase of $1.3 billion while Arbitrum decreased by $4.3 billion. On the contrary, Ethereum saw a more significant $30.6 billion decline.

In short, Tron’s [TRX] stablecoin dominance may be taking down Ethereum. It is also making inroads into other major Layer 1 networks. According to AMBCrypto, with increasing liquidity advantages, TRX is an altcoin to watch when the market moves back into a risk-on environment.

The real impact of this divergence becomes clearer when looking at on-chain activity.

Interestingly, data from the Tron network also seemed to confirm the stablecoins trend. Within a 24-hour period, Tron processed 12,990,075 transactions. This is the highest daily total for the past 7 days, averaging at around 150 transactions per second (TPS). This suggested that there may be consistent demand on the network, with the increase in stablecoins on Tron being due to real demand.

The result? The TRX/ETH ratio has risen by over 23% so far in Q4 and was only 15% away from making up all of its Q3 losses. On top of that, with stablecoin liquidity and on-chain activity between the two projects increasing, this trend may be accelerating.

If this continues, a break above the 0.002-resistance level could become more likely, opening the door for further TRX outperformance against ETH.

Taken together, with increasing stablecoin dominance, high volume, and the 23% rally in the TRX/ETH ratio, 0.002 is a key level. If it breaks through it could be a testement to TRX’s strength, while a failure to clear resistance may leave the pair trading within its current range.

As it stands, the combination of stablecoin inflows and growing network activity means that TRON is well-positioned to outperform Ethereum [ETH] this quarter.

Tron gained significant stablecoin share as Ethereum saw heavier outflows and weaker on-chain activity.

At press time, TRX/ETH was up 23% in Q4, with 0.002 as the next key resistance level.

#5152. Anthropic’s new AI leads freelance benchmarks, yet fails 58% of the tasks

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 tops a freelance work benchmark at 41.3%, meeting professional standards on roughly two in five projects.

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 ranks first on the Remote Labor Index, a test of complete freelance projects, with 41.3% of its deliverables judged to meet a professional reference standard.

Scale’s Nov. 12 leaderboard lists Fable 5.2 at 41.3%, ahead of the next-highest model, Opus 5.5 at 21.25%. Anthropic reported the result in its Nov. 9 release announcement, labeling the RLI score “High effort” and indicating that safeguards were enabled.

The original RLI paper, submitted in October 2025 by researchers from the Center for AI Safety and Scale AI, describes a dataset of 240 projects across 23 freelance work categories. Each includes a brief, the files needed to complete the task, and a professional human-reference deliverable.

The selected projects include documents, audio, video, 3D models, and computer-aided design files. That range makes file production and finished-product quality part of the challenge, alongside understanding what the client requested.

The researchers’ original evaluation method uses trained human reviewers to inspect the AI’s work against the brief and reference deliverable.

Independent evaluations and majority voting determine outcomes, and a project passes when the AI’s deliverable satisfies the brief at least as well as the reference, at a quality a reasonable client would accept.

A useful prototype or a partial result can therefore fall below the pass threshold, even when it demonstrates relevant skills. Passing demands a deliverable that clears the professional reference standard.

#5151. XRP, ETH, DOGE: Tim Draper Predicts Bitcoin to Absorb Altcoins

Billionaire and longtime Bitcoin (BTC) supporter Tim Draper predicts a future where Bitcoin absorbs the innovations of other altcoins.

Draper's statement has major implications for big cryptocurrencies like XRP, Ethereum (ETH) and Dogecoin (DOGE), which have built their own ecosystems and now compete in the wider cryptocurrency market.

In a recent post on X, Draper compared Bitcoin's rising dominance to Microsoft's path to becoming the dominant software company, as the company absorbed successful innovations from other businesses.

"Microsoft never invented the spreadsheet, word processor, or slide deck. They absorbed them," stated Draper.

The billionaire investor brought up pioneering software like Lotus 1-2-3 and WordPerfect, which eventually saw their dominance give way to Microsoft's Excel and Word.

Draper predicts that this could apply to the cryptocurrency industry, as Bitcoin could become the ultimate absorber of innovations from alternative blockchain networks.

"Altcoins are the Lotus 1-2-3s and WordPerfects of this time," wrote Draper.

Smaller cryptocurrencies are thus essentially incubators for technological innovations that could eventually become part of the bigger Bitcoin ecosystem, according to Draper.

"They show that it works, then the innovation that pulls the strongest gets ported to the Bitcoin platform," he added.

Draper also supported his statement by citing the rising market share of Bitcoin in the overall cryptocurrency market.

The billionaire investor recalled observing how Bitcoin dominance rose from about 40% to 50% and then even further to 61%.

"Bitcoin is that platform," said Draper, claiming that the biggest cryptocurrency could follow a path similar to Microsoft's.

This statement has implications for the future of big altcoins like Ethereum, XRP and Dogecoin.

Ethereum is the leading smart contract and dApp platform, while XRP is mainly known for its cross-border payments and the wider XRP Ledger ecosystem. Meanwhile, Dogecoin is one of the most iconic meme coins in the cryptocurrency market.

Still, the growing ecosystem around Bitcoin, which adds more scaling and programmability through solutions such as the Lightning Network and projects like Stacks, shows how Bitcoin is trying to expand beyond its basic use case as a cryptocurrency.

#5150. OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

After weeks of rejections from corporate America, OKX and ICE’s tokenized stock venue has found its launch lineup: crypto’s own public companies.

OKXICE said Wednesday that its Tokenized Securities Venue will open Nov. 23, letting eligible U.S. customers trade tokenized securities around the clock in the OKX app.

More than 70 assets are expected to be listed, including securities from Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com, plus 59 ETFs from Direxion. Strategy will also list its preferred stocks.

That’s a very different roster from the one the venue started with. As of its Nov. 5 notice, 67 companies, including every member of the “Magnificent Seven” tech giants, had objected to having their shares traded on the platform. Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy were notably absent from that list, and all three are now among the venue’s launch issuers.

“The SEC has created a real opening for American markets to lead the next era of finance,” said former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE, a joint venture between OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange.

Securitize will provide the tokenization infrastructure. Each token represents an entitlement backed one-for-one by a real U.S. share, carrying dividends, voting rights and other shareholder benefits, according to the announcement.

Trading will run through automated market maker pools built on Uniswap v4 on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2 network, and customers will hold their tokens in self-custodial wallets.

Strategy CEO Phong Le called tokenization on the venue “a natural next step” for the company’s preferred stocks, such as STRC.

Access is limited to eligible U.S.-based OKX customers who activate a CeDeFi Wallet, pass identity verification and complete onboarding with OKXICE. The venue operates under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate. Under the exemption, companies can keep third-party tokenized versions of their stock off a given venue by objecting within 30 days.

The launch caps a busy stretch for Wall Street tokenization. DTCC’s new tokenization service, which runs partly on the Canton Network, went live in late October, and Morgan Stanley said it was among the first banks to use it.

#5147. Solana treasury Forward Industries flashes a 30% SOL discount, with a catch

Forward Industries’ Solana treasury trades at a 29.7% NAV discount that narrows to 2.4% when potential share dilution is counted.

Forward Industries' Nov. 10 update puts the Solana treasury company's shares at a 29.7% discount to net assets using common shares alone.

Counting all the potential shares in the same disclosure, that discount shrinks to 2.4%. The difference is who could eventually share in the assets.

The share count is key almost as much as the treasury for investors comparing Nasdaq-listed FWDI with buying Solana directly.

The capitalization update uses a $5.53 share price and $600.2 million of net asset value (NAV). Forward's accompanying 8-K says the figures are preliminary, unaudited, and incomplete.

Dividing the same net assets among more potential shares produces three distinct comparisons:

Each discount is calculated as 1 minus the share price divided by NAV per share, using unrounded values.

The middle count already recognizes substantial claims beyond issued common stock: 12.27 million vested warrants with a displayed exercise price of $0.00, 689,010 options and 1.05 million unvested restricted stock units. It includes unvested options as well as vested ones.

Using the displayed prices for the two in-the-money option categories would add about $3.4 million if both groups vested and exercised for cash. With price, share count, and everything else fixed, that lifts the calculated discount to about 17.3%.

The largest addition between the middle and broadest counts is 13.38 million advisor and lead-investor warrants with a one-cent exercise price. Other additions include out-of-the-money options and warrants, plus 875,324 unvested performance stock units.

The accompanying prospectus to Forward's Nov. 9 filing describes three equal tranches requiring closing prices at 150%, 200% and 250% of the placement purchase price for 20 of 30 trading days after the relevant registration effectiveness.

Against the disclosed $18.50 purchase price, those trigger levels work out to $27.75, $37 and $46.25, subject to applicable adjustments.

#5146. A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

Bitcoin Core developers have disclosed three security vulnerabilities in the software that runs most of the Bitcoin network, including one high-severity bug that could disrupt mining, all of which have since been fixed.

Bitcoin Core, the reference software operating the majority of the network’s nodes, classifies vulnerabilities into four tiers, from “low” to “critical,” and these fell into the middle of that range.

The most serious, tracked as CVE-2025-62861, concerns block template creation, the process miners rely on to assemble new blocks.

According to the advisory, a chain reorganization could trigger an erroneous eviction of certain transactions, corrupting a node’s mempool, its waiting room for unconfirmed transactions, so that an affected node could no longer build block templates until it was restarted. For a miner, that means a halt to the very work that earns block rewards.

A second flaw, CVE-2025-62859, could let a peer stall a node’s block processing by announcing a compact block with a manipulated witness, a form of transaction malleation.

None of the three rises to the “critical” level Bitcoin Core reserves for bugs that threaten coin theft at the protocol level, unauthorized coin creation or permanent network-wide chain splits. The developers did not report any evidence the flaws were exploited.

The disclosures come amid a broader reckoning over how quickly Bitcoin’s defenders can keep pace with a shifting threat landscape.

Bitcoin Core recently rewrote its security policy to account for AI, adding a category for “easily discoverable” vulnerabilities that can be surfaced through a simple AI prompt, which it now patches and discloses immediately rather than holding under a long embargo.

That shift reflects mounting concern that large language models have lowered the bar for finding flaws, a worry underscored by the volunteer “Bitcoin Red Team,” which has been racing to uncover AI-discoverable bugs across the ecosystem before attackers can exploit them.

The fixes themselves shipped earlier, with the advisories following under Bitcoin Core’s disclosure timeline. Node operators are generally urged to run the latest software to stay protected.

#5141. What Are Solana Privacy Rings? Inside Helius’ Privacy Protocol for Solana

Every transaction on Solana is public by default. Anyone can look up a wallet address and see its balance, what it has sent and received, and when. That transparency is useful for verifying the network, but a problem for anyone who doesn't want their finances on display: a business paying salaries, a trader building a position, or an individual who simply doesn't want strangers seeing their savings.

Rings are programmable, onchain environments for encrypting money, markets, and finance - without ever leaving Solana

Now live on mainnet. pic.twitter.com/ezCIUCjcbI

Rings support SOL and other tokens on Solana, including SPL and Token-2022 tokens. Users keep custody of their funds throughout, since every transaction needs the owner's signature.

Crucially, the private balances live on the same network as everything else. Private transactions execute directly on Solana and encrypted data is stored on Solana's ledger, with no separate layer-2 network or sequencer in between. That means private balances sit alongside the same stablecoins, tokenized assets and DeFi liquidity as public ones.

Each private transfer comes with a zero-knowledge proof, a piece of cryptography that lets the network confirm the transfer is valid, for example that the sender actually had the funds, without revealing the numbers involved. Rings use a type of zero-knowledge proof called a Groth16 zk-SNARK, which Helius says can be generated in tens of milliseconds, either by a service provider or on the user's own device. Each private transfer settles in a single Solana transaction.

Users read their own encrypted balances with a viewing key. They can decrypt their balance locally, or let a service provider decrypt it and serve it to them, and they can share a key for a single transaction with an auditor or counterparty without exposing the rest of their history.

Moving funds into a Ring from an ordinary public wallet, or back out again, is visible onchain, including the asset and amount. But users who on-ramp from fiat currency straight into a private balance, through a supported provider, keep the asset and amount hidden from the start, according to Helius.

Because Custom Rings interoperate with the default Ring, regulated firms can keep their activity confidential from the market while still sharing the same liquidity as everyone else.

Private transfers are just the start. Solana programs can hold encrypted balances in private escrows and release them when set conditions are met, opening the way for:

At launch, Decrypt, mining and staking app ORE and cross-chain service Aurora Intents were using the default Ring, while Backpack, DFlow, Jupiter, Squads and Titan were among the teams working on integrations.

Helius says the core programs and zero-knowledge circuits have been audited by security firms Certora, OtterSec and ZK Security. Engineers from the Solana Foundation, Blueshift, Zcash and other teams took part in the "trusted setup," the one-off ceremony that generates the parameters the proofs rely on.

The code is open source, published on GitHub under the Apache 2.0 license, so anyone can inspect how it works.

Formal verification, a mathematical check that the circuits behave exactly as intended, is still underway and expected to finish by the end of 2026. Solana's own documentation currently describes Rings as being in beta.

#5135. Bitcoin Faces First Back-to-Back Annual Losses in History, But There's a Catch

Bitcoin Faces First Back-to-Back Annual Losses in History, But There's a Catch

Bitcoin is flirting with a historical first that bulls probably don’t want to hear: Two consecutive years of losses, something the cryptocurrency has never experienced in its 18-year history.

The world's largest cryptocurrency finished 2025 down 6.34% and has lost another 15.65% so far in 2026, trading around $73,850 on November 10. If Bitcoin fails to recover above its annual opening price of roughly $87,500 before December 31, it will close two calendar years in the red for the first time.

Historical annual returns show Bitcoin has experienced several brutal crashes since its creation, but each negative calendar year has been followed by a positive one. Even the disastrous 2018 and 2022 bear markets failed to produce consecutive annual losses.

The historical pattern is clear. Its significance, however, deserves a closer look.

Bitcoin's previous annual crashes were considerably more painful than its current losing streak. The cryptocurrency plunged 73.56% in 2018 and another 64.27% in 2022, wiping out most of investors' holdings in each respective year.

By comparison, Bitcoin's 6.34% decline in 2025 was relatively mild. Add the roughly 16% loss recorded this year, and the combined decline comes to approximately 21% after accounting for compounding.

That's a fraction of the losses investors endured in previous crypto winters, despite the market potentially recording a longer sequence of negative annual returns.

Bitcoin's performance in 2024 also provides some perspective. The cryptocurrency surged more than 120% that year, meaning investors who bought at the beginning of 2024 would still be sitting on substantial gains despite the subsequent decline.

The picture changes considerably when measuring losses from the market's actual peak instead of calendar-year boundaries.

Bitcoin reached approximately $126,000 in October 2025 before crashing below $58,000 during 2026. That represented a decline exceeding 50%, a significantly more painful move than the 21% combined loss reflected in the two annual candlesticks.

Analysts have pointed to Bitcoin's increasingly institutional investor base, spot exchange-traded funds, and deeper market liquidity as potential explanations for its comparatively smaller bear-market drawdowns.

Those structural changes may help explain why Bitcoin's current market cycle looks different from previous ones, although they have not eliminated major price crashes.

There is also a distinction between the length of a losing streak and the severity of the losses. A market can decline slowly over several calendar years without inflicting the same damage as a single catastrophic year.

And Bitcoin has already demonstrated that a bad year doesn't necessarily translate into a prolonged downturn. The cryptocurrency gained roughly 92% in 2019 following its 2018 collapse and surged more than 155% in 2023 after its disastrous 2022 performance.

For now, Bitcoin has 50 days left in 2026 to avoid its first back-to-back annual losses. A third consecutive decline in 2027 would extend the streak further, but that scenario is still hypothetical.

At its November 10 price of approximately $73,850, Bitcoin would need to gain about 18.5% to recover its 2026 opening price and finish the year in positive territory.

#5134. Aave user loses $305,000 after Across Protocol website hijack

Bitquery's November 12 update estimates $401,900 across nine wallets, including about $305,000 from one Aave deposit token.

A depositor in Aave lost about $305,000 in a deposit token during the Nov. 10 hijack of the cross-chain bridge Across Protocol's website, according to a Bitquery investigation updated Nov. 12.

Bitquery links the loss to spending permissions requested through the compromised interface, exposing a lending position held outside the bridge.

Bitquery's revised trace estimates $401,900 taken from nine wallets across five blockchains, using prices at the time the assets were stolen, and the Aave depositor accounts for about 76% of the traced losses. The update adds a previously missed ether sweep.

Across Protocol said its domain was taken over on Nov. 10 through social engineering involving its registrar, Namecheap.

The asset taken from the largest wallet was aWETH, an interest-bearing token representing an Aave deposit. Aave's documentation explains that its deposit tokens can be transferred and that a signed permit can authorize another account to spend them.

In Bitquery's reconstruction, the fake site requested a permit, which the attacker then used to move the depositor's tokens, and that spending permission put the separate lending position within reach.

The first transfer transaction on Nov. 10 recorded an unlimited approval followed by about 115.23 aWETH sent to one wallet and 28.81 aWETH to another. Bitquery identifies those recipients as the attacker's wallet and the drainer's fee wallet, respectively, matching an 80/20 split.

The depositor's transfer history shows four pulls ending three minutes after the first. Bitquery says the owner revoked the approval a minute after the last pull, but the transfers had already happened by that point.
