#5075. Suiet wallet to shut down in latest exit from Sui ecosystem

The self-custodial wallet will keep its API services running through Nov. 14. Sui’s DeFi total value locked has fallen more than 22% over the past month.

Cryptocurrency wallet Suiet is shutting down, adding to a series of closures and service withdrawals in the Sui ecosystem.

Suiet, a self-custodial wallet built for the Sui blockchain, said in a notice that it will stop updating and supporting its wallet, with API services set to end after Nov. 14. The shutdown comes as the Sui network has seen a sharp decline in decentralized finance (DeFi) activity.

Sui’s total value locked (TVL) stood at about $413 million on Wednesday, down more than 22% over the past month, according to DefiLlama data. Data from Token Terminal shows that Sui averaged about 128,000 daily active users in October, down from 601,000 in November 2025 – or around a 79% drop.

Meanwhile, other recent closures have also raised concerns about the costs and challenges of keeping projects running on the Sui network.

“Sui’s problems seem to mostly be of an economic nature resulting from expensive engineering costs and low user volume rather than protocol failures,” Kadan Stadelmann, former chief technology officer of Komodo Platform, told Cointelegraph. “No flaw in the system’s core design has been found.”

Cointelegraph reached out to the Sui Foundation and Suiet for comment but had not received responses by publication.

Related: Sui DeFi protocol Full Sail to wind down after Switchboard incident

Suiet’s shutdown immediately follows the Sui-based hardware wallet project Suiball also announcing its closure on Nov. 10. Meanwhile wallet provider Phantom ended support for Sui in September.

DeFi project Full Sail also shut down following a security incident involving Switchboard that caused about $91,000 in losses.

Despite the decline in active users, transactions on Sui have increased, reaching 324 million in October, up from 101 million in November 2025, according to Token Terminal. However, more transactions do not necessarily mean more users, and wallet providers must still decide whether supporting the network is worth the cost, according to Stadelmann.

“Multi-chain wallets have expansive attack surfaces, and Sui’s approach adds costs for vendors who can take their business to less capital intensive chains,” he said. “Any individual failure of a multi-chain wallet is more a reflection of the risks rather than an indictment of any given protocol.”

Sui has also experienced four network outages in 2026, resulting in around 22 hours of combined downtime, according to reports published by the Sui Foundation.

The network stopped processing transactions for about six hours in January, followed by three more outages on May 28 and 29. The May disruptions were linked to software bugs, including problems introduced by a network update, the Sui Foundation said at the time.

By comparison, rival blockchain Solana has not recorded a network-wide outage since February 2024, when it went offline for nearly five hours. Meanwhile, Aptos, which uses the same underlying smart contract programming language as Sui, last reported a mainnet outage in October 2023.

Network outages are significant because they can prevent DeFi users from trading, transferring funds or managing positions, although the Sui Foundation said no user funds were at risk during its May disruptions.

#5074. Tether Gold issuer follows USDT to complete Big Four audit

Tether said KPMG U.S. inspected every gold bar backing XAUT, following the USDT issuer's first full financial audit in August.

Tether said Thursday that KPMG U.S. completed an independent audit of the financial statements of TG Commodities, the issuer of its gold-backed XAUT token.

A spokesperson for the Big Four firm subsequently confirmed to The Block that it issued an unqualified, or "clean," opinion on TG Commodities's financial statements in accordance with AICPA standards for the year ending Dec. 31, 2025.

In a blog post, Tether said KPMG physically inspected and counted every individual gold bar backing XAUT Tether Gold (XAUT), checking the identifying information on each one. As of Dec. 31, 2025, TG Commodities held 520,089.350 fine troy ounces of gold worth $2.246 billion, against 520,089.300 XAUT in circulation, according to the firm.

The gold belongs to token holders and is stored in Switzerland. Each XAUT represents "an undivided ownership interest in gold on specified bars," Tether said.

KPMG had already audited the 2025 accounts of USDT Tether (USDT) stablecoin issuer Tether International. Tether announced that audit in August, and a spokesperson for the accounting firm confirmed to The Block that it had issued an unqualified opinion. Before then, Tether's regular reserve reporting consisted of attestations covering its holdings and liabilities on a given date.

"Our strategy was deliberate: start with the largest and most complex businesses, USDT and XAUT, and set the standard for the industry," Tether Chief Financial Officer Simon McWilliams said on Thursday.

The company is working toward audits of the rest of its businesses, and the audited figures matched Tether's quarterly reserve reports, he said.

#5073. Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ OKX’s Tokenized Stock Venue Launches Without Big Tech

In September, after the CLARITY Act died in the Senate, the SEC created an innovation exemption letting venues trade tokenized versions of US stocks without waiting for new legislation. It came with one condition. A company that doesn’t want a third party minting tokens of its shares can object in writing within 30 days, and that’s the end of it.

Companies have been objecting ever since. The count at the OKXICE venue went from 22 in October to 50, then 56, and as of its November 5 notice, 67. Every member of the Magnificent Seven is on that list.

OKXICE is launching anyway, on November 23. The venue will list more than 70 assets, though 59 of them are Direxion ETFs. The individual stocks that made it through are Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com. Strategy is also tokenizing its preferred shares.

Securitize handles the tokenization, each token is backed one-for-one by a real share and carries dividends and voting rights, and trading runs through Uniswap v4 pools on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2.

So a venture between a global exchange and the parent of the New York Stock Exchange built a tokenized equities market, and what it opens with is seven crypto companies and a series of leveraged ETFs.

Notably, this news comes just a day after the NYSE picked Avalanche to handle clearing and settlement for its own 24/7 tokenized securities platform, with a fourth-quarter target for launch after about a year of testing. The NYSE has signed agreements with Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate to support issuer-sponsored tokenized securities and transfer-agent functions. When the company’s own share registry issues the token, there is nothing to veto.

DTCC is doing a version of this too, with a tokenization service that went live October 28 on the Canton Network carrying 20 participants including Bank of America, Citi, JPMorgan and Citadel Securities. Morgan Stanley has already run equities and Treasuries through it.

Progress is being made, slowly but surely. It just gets built slowly, through transfer agents and clearing houses, by the incumbents. Now we wait to see how the market reacts to these first launches…

#5071. MSCI confirms Strategy’s removal from indexes at end of November

Strategy, Metaplanet and Upexi are marked for deletion in MSCI’s final list, with changes taking effect after the Nov. 30 close.

Index provider MSCI has confirmed Bitcoin treasury company Strategy will be removed from its global equity indexes, following months of debate over whether crypto treasury firms qualify as operating businesses.

MSCI’s final list of affected securities, released as part of its November index review, lists “STRATEGY A” with the action “Deleted.” Japan’s Metaplanet and crypto treasury company Upexi are also marked for deletion, while Ether treasury firm Sharplink is on a watchlist.

Strategy shares fell 1.65% in after-hours trading on Wednesday.

The changes take effect after market close on Nov. 30, under the implementation timetable MSCI announced in October.

Removal could prompt funds tracking the affected benchmarks to sell the companies’ shares, reducing a source of passive investment demand.

As Cointelegraph reported in October, JPMorgan previously estimated that Strategy’s exclusion could trigger roughly $2.8 billion in selling.

Strategy opposed the proposal, arguing that it repackaged MSCI’s earlier effort to exclude digital asset treasury companies and injected policy judgments into index construction.

The company nevertheless played down the potential disruption. In its consultation response, Strategy said MSCI-linked funds held approximately 13 million shares, representing 3.1% of its basic shares outstanding and less than one day’s trading volume.

#5070. DOJ Wins Court Authority to Seize $1.2 Billion of North Korea’s Stolen Binance Crypto as Freezes Top $2.7 Billion

The DOJ said Thursday it has court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in cryptocurrency stolen from Binance, part of a freeze of more than $2.7 billion that officials said halted about half of North Korea’s October exchange heist.

In a Thursday announcement, the department said that on or about Oct. 29, members of the North Korean military, referred to by the FBI as TraderTraitor, stole more than $6 billion worth of virtual assets from Binance. That is below the $6.34 billion figure Binance has covered since the attack.

Binance said it had completed coverage of the assets hit in the breach, replacing the tokens from company funds rather than by recovering the stolen coins. Those are separate tracks: the DOJ is trying to interdict what was taken, while the exchange has said users were made whole from its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its treasury.

Assistant Attorney General A. Tysen Duva of the DOJ’s Criminal Division said the department and its partners were able to “immediately halt about half of this historic breach” by freezing stolen assets “before they got beyond law enforcement reach.” The freeze of more than $2.7 billion is a joint figure for U.S. government and private-sector efforts. Of that amount, the DOJ said it has already obtained court authorization to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion. The underlying court papers were not posted with the announcement.

The $1.2 billion is not newly recovered money but comes out of the roughly $2.85 billion Binance said on Nov. 3 had been frozen or intercepted, and Thursday’s court authorization is the legal step that lets the government move toward taking ownership of those funds through forfeiture, where until now they had simply been frozen at the token issuers.

Even that freeze is not the end of the trail. The DOJ said TraderTraitor actors are still moving the stolen assets through cryptocurrency bridges, mixers and exchanges, and that tracing, seizure and forfeiture work continues. Last week, on Oct. 31, the FBI, with the U.S. Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, issued a Cyber Alert attributing the theft to North Korea and urging virtual asset service providers to block transactions with or derived from addresses the actors are using. The alert said investigators have seen the stolen assets converted primarily into ether.

Separately from the DOJ and Binance case, the U.S. attorney’s office in the Central District of California and the FBI announced the seizure of cloud-based infrastructure used in a social engineering campaign the FBI is calling FaceStealer. The FBI said the scheme has resulted in the theft of more than $400 million in cryptocurrency over the last 18 months and has also been used in attempts to compromise people who maintain open-source software, a step the bureau said appears calculated to enable supply-chain attacks.

The method is a fake video call. The FBI said North Korean hackers, known publicly under names including APT38 and BlueNoroff, lure targets with meeting invitations sent from accounts stolen from earlier victims. Once a target joins, the hackers play a prerecorded webcam video of a person the target knows, or a deepfake of a well-known figure in the cryptocurrency industry, then introduce a fake technical error and send a “fix” that installs malware. In several cases, the theft came months to more than a year after the first compromise, as the hackers waited for wallet balances to grow.

A Thursday FBI FLASH report said the meetings run on imposter versions of Zoom, Microsoft Teams and Google Meet, with invitations often moving through hijacked Telegram, LinkedIn, Slack or Discord accounts. Pursuant to a federal warrant, the FBI seized more than 900 webcam videos of FaceStealer victims. The bureau has posted a hub for the campaign at fbi.gov/facestealer.

Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor tied both Thursday’s actions to the same charge: “North Korea’s government bankrolls its rogue regime and illicit weapons program through cryptocurrency theft.”

The Binance recovery is still being run by the FBI’s Los Angeles Field Office, Cyber Division and Virtual Asset Unit, with the Secret Service and IRS-CI. The DOJ did not say how much of the more than $2.7 billion freeze, or of the $1.2 billion under court authority, is bitcoin.

#5068. Unocoin ‘login suspension’ continues as exchange flags outgoing BitGo ETH transactions

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Unocoin says it lacks full visibility into its frozen BitGo wallets and estimates a server rebuild will take up to five working days.

Indian cryptocurrency exchange Unocoin has identified "outgoing transactions" from an ETH address in its BitGo wallet infrastructure, adding a new development to a security incident that has left customers unable to access their accounts since Nov. 9.

In its latest statement, the exchange said it discovered the activity on Thursday and immediately escalated the matter to BitGo, which has opened an investigation.

The exchange said the incident traces back to Nov. 6, when it first detected unauthorized activity on its infrastructure and initiated "precautionary measures'' to protect user funds.

"As part of these measures, we requested an immediate freeze of our enterprise accounts with BitGo and Fireblocks, which hold cryptocurrency balances required to facilitate our day-to-day crypto transactions," the exchange wrote.

The incident took another turn on Monday, when the exchange said it detected more unauthorized activity on its platform and suspended all services immediately in the "interest of protecting customer assets and data." This included implementing what it described as "login suspension."

Unocoin says more than 90% of customer cryptocurrency is held in cold storage and that no funds were lost in the incident. However, when asked by a user on X whether those could be recovered, the exchange replied that it would not "give you an assurance based on assumptions" until transaction-level details are verified.

The exchange also said it cannot log in to the frozen accounts and that BitGo's use of change addresses limits what it can check on-chain.

"We currently do not have complete visibility into all potentially unintended transactions across our BitGo wallets," it wrote in the incident report.

Unocoin said rebuilding its servers from the ground up would take an estimated four to five working days. The investigation into the breach, potential data exposure, and status of user holdings remains ongoing.

Meanwhile, Onchain Lens reported that Arkham shows $0 across 81,000 tracked Unocoin addresses and that the exchange's website returned a 504 error. The exchange has not confirmed that its tracked wallets were drained.

#5067. Binance CSO unwittingly foreshadowed $6.3B hack tactic months before attack

In a July interview, Binance chief security officer Jimmy Su explained how fake coding assessments could compromise employee devices, a tactic later used in the exchange’s record-breaking hack.

Months before Binance lost $6.3 billion to a hack, its chief security officer described to Cointelegraph how a fake job interview could give attackers a foothold inside the exchange — closely resembling one of the entry methods later used against the exchange.

During a July interview about Binance’s response to rising security threats, Jimmy Su told Cointelegraph that fake job interviews were “something we worry about a lot internally,” explaining how attackers could tailor a job opportunity to an employee before persuading them to run code as part of a technical assessment.

“And once that gets executed, the device is compromised and then they can start harvesting credentials,” Su said, adding that attackers could then use that to penetrate further into the network.

Su’s detailed warning about malicious coding assessments was not included in Cointelegraph’s July coverage, but takes on new significance following the biggest crypto exchange hack in history.

In September, a Binance software engineer downloaded a malicious coding assignment onto his work computer during a fake job interview. That compromise became part of the multistage attack that culminated in October’s theft, according to Binance’s preliminary investigation.

During the interview with Cointelegraph, Su had also anticipated why security tools might miss such a trap. A technical assessment could involve seemingly legitimate steps, such as downloading a code repository from GitHub, he explained in July.

“Each step is actually legitimate on its own. So a lot of the anti-virus tools may not be able to detect it and flag it,” he said.

Binance’s investigation subsequently described a similar detection failure, saying the assignment’s setup process executed malware that evaded its endpoint security tools through obfuscation and other evasion techniques.

Su told Cointelegraph that it was Binance’s responsibility to contain the risk when employees pursued outside opportunities on work computers.

“Of course employees can explore outside opportunities. What we do worry about is that they do so on the company device,” he said. If an employee fell for a fake interview, he added, “the onus is on us to detect it,” and to “stop the actual execution of that code.”

Related: North Korean hackers create memecoins to launder funds from Binance hack: Elliptic

Binance was already testing employees against fake recruiters. Su said its internal red team had been conducting monthly phishing simulations for three to four years, with remedial training for employees who failed.

“In the beginning, the security hygiene left a lot to be desired. But after this amount of time, the company has improved significantly,” he said.

Kevin Gibson of recruitment firm Proof of Search told Cointelegraph that the threat is also changing how some crypto employers assess prospective hires, with clients increasingly wanting to know whether candidates have been met in person.

“A number of our clients are now asking us straight up whether we’ve met a candidate in person,” Gibson said, adding that candidates personally met by a third-party recruiter have an advantage.

Employees, like in Binance’s case, can also become targets.

“In crypto, a dodgy job offer or a ‘quick catch-up call’ can be the first step in a multimillion-dollar hit,” he said.

Binance said it blocked 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats so far this year, which is roughly one attack every five days.

Following October’s attack, Binance said it had tightened device controls, rebuilt affected wallet systems and internal AI tooling, strengthened access controls and revoked compromised credentials.

It also said it was increasing investment in endpoint protection and developer-environment security, and engaging independent cybersecurity experts to review the incident. Some technical details and security measures would remain confidential, it said.

#5066. DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC), the backbone of U.S. securities settlement, plans to bring its tokenized securities to Solana, it announced on Thursday.

The firm will connect the DTCC Tokenization Service (DTS) to the Solana network, allowing eligible assets held by its Depository Trust Company (DTC) subsidiary to be converted into tokens on Solana. The integration is planned for the second half of 2027.

BREAKING: @The_DTCC to tokenize custodied assets on Solana. announced plans to connect the DTCC Tokenization Service to Solana, enabling the tokenization of eligible DTC-custodied assets on the network.

The integration is planned for the second half of 2027, further advancing… pic.twitter.com/gXszWFgUOX

"DTCC is bringing regulated, DTC-tokenized securities to another major blockchain ecosystem, giving market participants greater choice in where they access and utilize tokenized assets," DTCC CEO Frank La Salla said.

DTS went live on October 28 on the Canton Network and DTCC's own private blockchain, built on the Besu framework, with more networks due to be added in 2027, according to DTCC's launch announcement. The service creates tokenized "digital twins" of securities held at DTC, which custodies more than $114 trillion in assets. Solana joins Stellar and Circle's Arc blockchain, which are expected to be added in 2027, according to a DTCC fact sheet.

Under a no-action letter from the SEC, the service is limited to a defined set of highly liquid assets, including Russell 1000 stocks, ETFs tracking major indexes, and U.S. Treasuries. Morgan Stanley said last week it had tokenized U.S. stocks and Treasuries through the service.

Tokens issued on Solana "will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," said Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services.

Solana has been actively courting traditional finance in recent months, with Solana Foundation head of institutional Nick Ducoff arguing that, "Institutions choose Solana because it already runs real financial activity at scale." He added that institutions are "already issuing, settling and deploying capital" on the network, in a tweet shared by Solana's official account.

Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, and the network completed its Alpenglow upgrade, cutting the time it takes for transactions to become final to roughly 150 milliseconds.

Solana is also lining up more venues for tokenized stocks. Trading platform Formation has notified the SEC of plans to trade tokenized U.S. stocks on Solana, starting with Galaxy and Forward Industries.

#5064. South Korea to permit crypto withdrawal freezes during hacking threats

The move follows recent security incidents involving South Korean crypto exchanges, including GOPAX’s precautionary suspension of services and a $70,000 hot-wallet breach at Prabang.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) plans to allow crypto exchanges to suspend deposits and withdrawals when hacking is suspected, rather than waiting until a breach is confirmed, amid a push for stronger industry security.

On Thursday, the FSC said it would issue a no-action letter permitting preventive suspensions under certain conditions. Current rules prohibit exchanges from blocking withdrawals without legitimate grounds and explicitly allow restrictions only when a hacking incident is clearly established.

The regulator also called for round-the-clock monitoring and incident response, higher cold-wallet holdings, and stronger insurance coverage and reserves. The FSC said exchanges unable to monitor continuously would process deposits and withdrawals only during staffed hours and notify users in advance.

The move follows recent security incidents involving South Korean exchanges. On Friday, crypto exchange GOPAX suspended trading, deposits and standard withdrawals after receiving a hacking threat, although a representative said no breach had occurred. On Oct. 24, trading platform Prabang suspended services after detecting abnormal hot-wallet withdrawals, reporting about $70,000 in losses.

In October, the FSC identified cybersecurity deficiencies at 130 of 555 financial companies reviewed nationwide. It separately identified GOPAX operator Streami and Prabang among 16 companies involved in earlier security incidents.

#5062. Visa adds Western Union’s USDPT stablecoin to its settlement network in Anchorage tie-up

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Visa and eligible partners can now settle VisaNet obligations using Western Union's USDPT, issued by Anchorage Digital Bank.

Anchorage Digital said it is collaborating with Visa to support select stablecoins issued by its federally chartered bank as native settlement assets on the Visa network, starting with Western Union's USDPT.

Visa and eligible partners can use the token to settle VisaNet obligations, with the companies also creating a framework to consider additional Anchorage-issued stablecoins, according to a statement shared with The Block.

"Stablecoins have made money move faster, but speed alone won't bring institutions onchain," Anchorage Digital CEO Nathan McCauley said. "They need a regulated institution behind the money. With Anchorage Digital select issued stablecoins now being supported on the Visa network, institutions get both: the speed of a stablecoin and the safety of a federally regulated bank behind it."

USDPT debuted in May on Solana, with Anchorage Digital Bank handling issuance and Fireblocks providing the wallet and settlement infrastructure. Western Union initially launched the stablecoin in the Philippines and Bolivia, with plans to use it for 24/7 settlements across its network of more than 200 countries.

Anchorage has also issued stablecoins for Tether (USAT), OSL Group (USDGO), Ethena Labs (USDtb), Falcon Finance (fUSD), and Zelle (ZLUSD).

Anchorage reached a valuation of more than $3 billion in its $350 million Series D funding round in 2021. It has spent this year expanding its institutional services, including onchain lending through Spark and custody-based collateral management.

Anchorage said the partnership with Visa fits its broader push to become a settlement bank for stablecoins, extending its business beyond issuance into custody and settlement services for institutions.

The partnership comes as stablecoin supply contracts. According to The Block's data dashboard, the current total supply has fallen to around $280 billion, down from $300 billion in March this year. Tether's USDT dominates the sector with $177 billion, followed by Circle's USDC at around $70 billion.

For Visa, the Anchorage partnership builds on an expanding stablecoin settlement network. The company said in September that its annualized stablecoin settlement run rate reached $20 billion, more than 15 times the year-earlier figure.

Visa has also added networks including Monad, Canton, Base, and Tempo to its settlement options.

#5061. BlackRock Bitcoin ETF outflows hit $2B over nine sessions

US spot Bitcoin ETF outflows rose to nearly $300 million on Wednesday, up from $95 million a day earlier, as five-session withdrawals reached $1.44 billion.

BlackRock’s spot Bitcoin exchange-traded fund (ETF), the iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), recorded more than $2 billion in net outflows over nine consecutive trading sessions through Wednesday.

Investors pulled a net $269.7 million from IBIT on Wednesday, accounting for about 90% of the day’s $300 million in net withdrawals across US spot Bitcoin ETFs, according to Farside Investors data.

Grayscale’s Bitcoin Trust ETF (GBTC) recorded $39.1 million in net withdrawals, while the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust ETF (MSBT) drew $9 million in net inflows.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs posted nearly $1.44 billion in net withdrawals over five straight sessions. Wednesday’s increase followed three sessions of easing outflows.

Crypto exchange Bitfinex said on Wednesday that slowing ETF outflows could be an early step toward a market floor, but were not enough on their own to establish a recovery.

Bitfinex found that in the ETF outflow streaks it studied earlier this year, Bitcoin gained a median 6.4% over 10 trading sessions when the streaks ended with at least three consecutive sessions of net inflows.

Bitcoin traded near $73,000 at the time of writing, down 8.5% over the past week, according to CoinGecko.

In a Wednesday note shared with Cointelegraph, Laser Digital’s derivatives trading desk pointed to weak spot demand, a persistently negative Coinbase premium and large liquidations of leveraged long positions.

The desk said Bitcoin barely rose during Monday’s rebound in the Nasdaq, which gained almost 3%.

#5059. SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

SoFi is widening the reach of its digital dollar, bringing SoFiUSD, the first stablecoin issued by a U.S. national bank, onto Polygon’s payments infrastructure as banks and fintechs push stablecoins deeper into everyday money movement.

Polygon Labs said Thursday that SoFiUSD is now live on Polygon Open Money Stack, a payments layer that bundles fiat on- and off-ramps, embedded wallets and cross-chain settlement into a single integration.

The move adds Polygon alongside Ethereum and Solana, the networks SoFiUSD already runs on, and lets SoFi’s more than 17 million members buy, sell, hold, and convert the token on Polygon directly in the SoFi app, without opening a separate crypto wallet or manually bridging assets between chains.

“SoFi is well positioned to bring stablecoins into mainstream finance because it combines the regulatory foundation of a national bank with the reach of a major consumer and enterprise technology platform,” said Marc Boiron, CEO of Polygon Labs.

The expansion lands as stablecoins move beyond crypto trading into the plumbing used to settle card payments, pay businesses, and send money across borders.

On Polygon Open Money Stack, SoFiUSD joins USDC, USDT and PayPal’s PYUSD, and can connect to the wallets, banking rails, and local currencies businesses need for payouts, settlement and cross-border transfers, opening up uses well beyond consumer trading.

The token is redeemable one-to-one for U.S. dollars and issued by SoFi Bank, which is regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

SoFi launched SoFiUSD late last year across Ethereum and Solana as the first stablecoin from a U.S. national bank, and it already settles the company’s entire card program over Mastercard’s network. The firm has been building out a broader digital-money suite, recently launching SoFi Earn, a tokenized deposit that pays 4.40% yield and converts one-to-one into SoFiUSD.

For Polygon, the addition deepens a payments business that has become one of the network’s calling cards.

Polygon recently extended Open Money Stack to Solana, and the chain has processed more than $3 trillion in lifetime stablecoin transfer volume, according to the company. Visa and Mastercard support stablecoin settlement on Polygon, while Stripe uses it to route USDC payments for merchants in more than 150 countries.

SoFiUSD is a payment stablecoin, not a bank deposit, and is not FDIC- or SIPC-insured, bank-guaranteed or legal tender.

#5057. Exodus’ ‘cost reset is real’ as payments offset crypto wallet slowdown, StoneX says

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Exodus targets $30 million in annual savings as StoneX keeps its $12 price target and sees adjusted breakeven on the horizon.

StoneX kept a Buy rating and a $12 price target for Exodus Movement Exodus Movement, Inc. (EXOD) on Thursday.

The basis of its argument is that Exodus' push into payments and lower costs are helping offset weaker activity in its legacy crypto wallet business, bringing it closer to breaking even on an adjusted basis. "The cost reset is real," StoneX analyst Mark Palmer wrote in a Thursday report.

Exodus shares were up over 8% to $4.68 on Thursday morning, with StoneX's $12 target implying a further gain of around 150%.

The company reported third-quarter earnings on Monday, reaching revenue of $32.5 million and topping analyst estimates of between $29 million and $31 million. Its adjusted EBITDA loss shrank to $3.9 million from $6.7 million in the second quarter, while its net loss totaled $8.3 million.

Exodus' payments segment contributed nearly $11 million in its first full quarter following the acquisitions of Monavate and Baanx in May. That helped offset a 29% year-over-year drop-off in wallet-related revenue to $21.6 million. The company noted that around 64% of payments revenue came from traditional payments, while the other 36% came from onchain finance.

Exodus also cut its headcount from 326 to 214 employees between May and Monday's earnings, with management expecting roughly $30 million in savings going into 2027.

"Our cost base is lower. If exchange volumes rise, revenue can grow without a proportionate increase in overhead," CEO JP Richardson said on Monday's earnings call.

However, Richardson also warned that many of the company's milestones still depend on regulatory approval and execution.

Exodus is still waiting on approval in Latvia that would allow the company to resume issuing its payment cards in Europe. The company said it has already resumed issuing cards in the U.S.

#5056. Ethlabs drops Hegotá slot proposal in push for 2027 quantum readiness

Ethlabs’ roadmap aims to help Ethereum withstand quantum attacks and give users a way to protect their accounts by 2027.

Ethereum research group Ethlabs said Wednesday it will withdraw its proposal to speed up the network’s block production from the planned Hegotá upgrade, urging core developers to prepare the network for quantum attacks by 2027.

On Wednesday, the group outlined “pqai27,” a proposed roadmap that aims to protect Ethereum against threats from quantum computing and increasingly capable artificial intelligence. It said unrelated upgrades should be reconsidered.

Ethlabs previously backed Ethereum Improvement Proposal (EIP)-8198, known as Quick Slots, which would initially cut Ethereum’s slot time from 12 seconds to 10 seconds. The group said it would redirect resources to pqai27 but will still pursue faster slots in 2028.

For Hegotá, Ethlabs said FOCIL, a planned censorship-resistance feature, should proceed only if it does not jeopardize quantum readiness. The group called Frame Transactions, which would enable more flexible account authentication, central to the proposed transition.

It said Glamsterdam, the upgrade before Hegotá, should proceed as planned.

Ethereum core developers have not approved the proposal. The Ethereum Foundation’s published roadmap still targets December 2029 for quantum resistance. At Devcon, researcher Justin Drake said he hopes to complete the transition in 2028.

#5053. Tron and Tether bring in Chainalysis after T3 crime unit folds

The group will focus on illicit USDT activity on Tron and help law enforcement investigate suspicious transfers and freeze funds faster.

Tron, Tether and Chainalysis announced a task force Thursday to identify and disrupt illicit activity involving USDT (USDT) stablecoin on the Tron blockchain.

The Digital Crime Task Force will use blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis’ data and AI tools to flag suspicious activity and give law enforcement leads to trace and freeze funds, according to an announcement shared with Cointelegraph.

Bloomberg reported on Nov. 4 that the T3 Financial Crime Unit, which Tron and Tether formed with TRM Labs in 2024, disbanded in late August. An unnamed source cited by the outlet said slow responses and poor coordination hampered the unit.

Cointelegraph contacted Tether, Tron and TRM Labs for comment but did not receive responses by publication time.

Related: Binance CSO unwittingly foreshadowed $6.3B hack tactic months before attack

The task force’s priorities include investment and romance scams, crypto theft, terrorist financing, North Korean cyber operations and cartel money laundering.

“Criminals are using AI to move faster than ever, and waiting for a victim to report a crime is no longer good enough,” Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said in the announcement. He put Tether’s cumulative USDT freezes at more than $7 billion.

USDT on Tron accounted for 99.9% of the $1.07 billion in crypto received by 34 casinos in Sihanoukville, a Cambodian city known as a hub for scam compounds, from Jan. 1 to Sept. 7, TRM Labs reported.

Casinos tied to US-sanctioned networks received $687 million of those inflows. TRM Labs cited low fees and deep liquidity as reasons for the widespread use of USDT on Tron in the region.

Following the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, stolen funds were converted to USDT on Tron and cashed out through suspected Chinese over-the-counter trading services, according to blockchain intelligence company Elliptic.

During the record $6.3 billion Binance hack on Oct. 29, hackers swapped approximately $234 million in USDT for Tron’s native token (TRX) before Tether froze the remaining USDT in two wallets linked to the theft, according to Crystal Intelligence.

The US Federal Bureau of Investigation attributed both hacks to North Korea. In a statement published by North Korean state media on Nov. 3, Kim Yo Jong rejected the Binance attribution and cited Bybit as another case in which she said Washington tried to blame Pyongyang.

#5052. Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of Nvidia, ending a 33-year tenure at the helm of the chipmaker he co-founded, after reports of affairs with a suspected Chinese spy and a senior company executive.

Huang has stepped down as president, CEO and a member of the board, effective immediately, Nvidia said on Thursday. Colette Kress, Nvidia's chief financial officer since 2013, will serve as interim CEO while remaining CFO, as the board searches for a permanent successor from both inside and outside the company. Nvidia did not give a reason for Huang's resignation, but thanked him for his "immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

NVIDIA announces CEO transition. Read the release: https://t.co/MTTUvFHCJA

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 12, 2026

The board also named Harvey Jones, a former CEO and executive chairman of chip design software company Synopsys, as its first-ever chair.

"The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption," Jones said.

Kress said she was "committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years." Nvidia will report its third-quarter results as scheduled on Tuesday, the company said.

Nvidia shares were down 7.7% at $160.28 shortly after the market opened on Thursday, having fallen as low as $148.80, according to Yahoo! Finance data.

Huang's departure comes four days after he acknowledged in a letter to staff that he had been unfaithful to his wife, prompting Nvidia's board to open an independent investigation. He had told the board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed, The New York Times reported.

The Times had reported in October that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, while the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said the two had been romantically involved. China has denied the allegations.

On Wednesday, the Times reported that Huang had also had a relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care, who reports to him. The Wall Street Journal later reported that the board was preparing to oust him, adding to the pressure on the CEO.

The scandal has wiped hundreds of billions of dollars off Nvidia's market value, with the company losing about $430 billion in two days earlier this week. Nvidia and Huang also face a proposed shareholder class action, and lawmakers had threatened to subpoena him.

Huang ran Nvidia with about 60 executives reporting directly to him and has said he did not believe in succession planning, leaving the company without an obvious successor.

Prior to the announcement of Huang's resignation, one person inside the company had told the Financial Times that naming Kress as interim CEO would be the simplest option. Other potential candidates cited by the paper's sources include Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia's data center business, and Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive.

Huang remains Nvidia's largest shareholder, with a stake of about 3.6%, according to the Journal.

#5050. Bitcoin falls to 12-week low as Jensen Huang’s exit from Nvidia rattles markets

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Huang resigned as the company's president, CEO and board member effective immediately, while MSCI plans to remove bitcoin treasury firm Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell to its lowest level in nearly 12 weeks on Thursday, trading as low as $71,200, as markets reacted to Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's abrupt departure and continued uncertainty surrounding potential selling pressure from MSCI index removals.

Bitcoin was down 3.4% to $71,600 at publication time. Other major crypto assets, including Ethereum, Solana and XRP, also declined more than 3.5% over the past 24 hours.

More than $750 million in crypto positions were liquidated in the past 24 hours, including over $634 million in long positions, according to CoinGlass.

"We remain cautious on BTC as it tests $73k, the level we flagged as first potential support, with no convincing signs of size stepping in at these prices," Jasper De Maere, OTC trader at Wintermute, said Thursday morning. "Some bluechip names are seeing activity, but without large buyers the majors need ETF flows to carry them. That changes if flows turn structurally positive for several days in a row."

Meanwhile, MSCI confirmed it will remove Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30, raising the prospect of selling in the bitcoin proxy. Wintermute described the move as "a passive-selling risk on a BTC proxy where we have no size estimate."

Nvidia's leadership turmoil had been building over the past week. The company launched an investigation into Huang last weekend following reports that he had been a "target of a foreign intelligence effort" amid an alleged affair with a Chinese spy. On Wednesday, the New York Times reported that Huang was also having an affair with a woman who directly reports to him.

On Thursday morning, Huang resigned as president, CEO, and board member, effective immediately. CFO Colette Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor. Kress joined Nvidia in 2013 and will remain CFO during the transition.

President Donald Trump also weighed in, saying former Apple CEO Tim Cook should take the helm of the AI chip developer. "[T]here is only one man to take his place," Trump wrote on Truth Social. "A Legend replaces a Legend!"

Nvidia shares opened Thursday down 12%, extending their decline this month. Nvidia perpetual futures on Hyperliquid generated $2.34 billion in 24-hour trading volume.

"I have a lot of respect for Jensen; he's built an incredible company," Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong said on CNBC as news of his resignation broke. "It's sad to see and hopefully he gets a moment to reflect on [his decisions]."

Meanwhile, Binance cofounder Changpeng Zhao said it's a "mistake for Nvidia to let Jensen resign."

#5048. Stablecoin supply falls $14.2B in biggest 30-day drop since June 2022

Crypto markets were left with “less fuel” to rally after the market cap of major stablecoins saw its biggest 30-day drop in over four years.

Stablecoin supply fell by $14.2 billion over 30 days, the biggest drop since June 2022, according to Glassnode.

The rolling 30-day contraction in stablecoin supply hit $14.2 billion this week, more than the $9.5 billion drop seen as Bitcoin (BTC) fell below $58,000 on July 1. It also marks the most pronounced drawdown since June 2022, when supply fell by nearly $30 billion.

Selected stablecoin supply data. Source: Glassnode

Stablecoin liquidity is a key yardstick for gauging crypto market sentiment.

“A shrinking supply means holders are redeeming stablecoins for dollars and taking that cash out of the market, which leaves less fuel for a rally,” Glassnode commented.

USDT, the largest stablecoin by market cap, continues to trade slightly below its $1 peg, per data from TradingView. Glassnode attributes the phenomenon to the “same pressure” from investors rotating out of the stablecoin market.

“Should the 30-day change turn back to growth, it would be the first sign that new money is coming in,” it added.

USDT/USD one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto market metrics continue to point to weakening demand as Bitcoin trades near 11-week lows.

Related: Bitcoin short-term holders send 47K BTC to exchanges at a loss

On Tuesday, onchain analytics platform CryptoQuant that its measure of Bitcoin demand fell by 284,000 BTC over 30 days, the most pronounced downturn since March 2025.

#5047. I Work Out for a Living—Here Are the Best Headphones for Everything From Runs to Gym Sessions

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Because every sweat session needs an epic soundtrack.

WHETHER YOU PREFER to listen to music, a podcast, or tune into sports while getting your sweat on, you'll need a solid pair of workout headphones to make it happen. Beyond a high-quality pre-workout, nothing elevates your training quite like the perfect pair of headphones. These devices not only drown out grunting gym-goers but also help you stay focused, find your rhythm, and push through those last few reps when your body is telling you to quit.

The key to a good pair of workout headphones is finding ones that fit your ears snugly and won't budge while you run or do kettlebell swings. Workout headphones need water resistance to handle sweat and 5-mile runs in the rain. We can recommend eleven pairs of workout headphones for a range of training environments.

Like our favorite running shoes, modern workout headphones are built for performance, combining durability with athletic-minded features. Sweat and water resistance are non-negotiable, while a secure, comfortable fit prevents them from falling out during burpees, sprints, or heavy lifts. Meanwhile, battery life has improved dramatically in recent years. Many models offer enough juice to power through a week’s worth of workouts on a single charge. Need to focus? Noise cancellation, intuitive controls, and quick-pairing technology keep you in the zone rather than fumbling with your tech.

Whether you’re just starting your fitness journey or a seasoned athlete, the best workout headphones can transform your training sessions. The Men’s Health fitness and tech editors tested a few dozen options to identify the eleven best models available. From true wireless earbuds to over-ear models to bone conduction headphones, these top-performing headphones deliver impressive durability, high-quality sound, and workout-specific features to help you push through the pain.

The AirPods Pro have improved significantly over the past few updates, and the latest version, the AirPods Pro 3, is what we believe are the best do-it-all workout headphones. These earbuds deliver excellent comfort, premium features, and high-quality sound. Apple has systematically addressed every pain point we experienced with earlier models, transforming these wireless earbuds from casual earbuds into essential fitness companions.

The fit is a major reason the AirPods Pro work so well during workouts, even surpassing the latest Apple release, the Apple AirPods 5. After dozens of reps, we found they don’t budge. The multiple silicone ear tips let our testing team find the perfect seal during burpees and box jumps. Despite wearing them for multiple training sessions (and in the office), we experienced no ear fatigue thanks to their lightweight design.

The improved ergonomics also contribute to better passive noise isolation, working with active noise cancellation to block out excessively loud gym music. For athletes who rely on music for motivation during workouts, the secure, comfortable fit eliminates the constant annoyance of loose earbuds that need readjusting. Their IP57 rating provides additional peace of mind, ensuring they can handle the sweat that comes with serious workouts.

MH Senior Fitness Editor Brett Williams, NASM, found the most exciting new feature of the AirPods Pro 3s to be their ability to monitor your heart rate through your ear canals, a capability more commonly associated with devices that wrap around your wrist or chest. They aren't the first headphones to do so, but having this added to Apple's already impressive offerings only strengthens their case.

Battery life exceeded our expectations, with up to eight hours of continuous playback and a charging case that extends total listening time to 24 hours. The case supports wireless charging, making it easy to recharge on a charging pad. Meanwhile, the AirPods Pro 3 support several advanced features like adaptive audio, conversation awareness, and transparency mode, the latter of which lets outside sound in, so you can be aware of what’s going on around you during a run.

The touch controls are intuitive during workouts—you can play/pause, skip tracks, answer a call, and adjust the volume by pressing the stem. For iPhone owners, the AirPods are a cinch to set up. Simply open the case next to your phone, tap Connect, and you’re ready to go. This combination of comfort, performance, and ease of use makes the AirPods Pro 3 the best overall workout headphones, setting a high bar for others to match.

Read More: Our Review of the Apple AirPods Pro 3s

While the Beats Studio Pro aren’t specifically designed for working out, they’re by no means out of place in a gym. The headphones maintain the sleek, recognizable Beats aesthetic and bass-heavy sound the brand is known for, while offering premium features that make them great for training and office use.

The over-ear design provides top-notch sound isolation through plush memory foam ear cushions, while the adjustable headband distributes weight evenly across your head. They balance comfort with stability—the headband applies just enough pressure to keep the headphones secure without becoming uncomfortable during extended wear. During testing, the Studio Pro remained firmly in place through moderate workouts like weightlifting and walks, though they’re not ideal for high-impact exercises.

Battery life is fantastic, lasting up to 40 hours with ANC off (24 hours with ANC on). When the battery gets low, you can get up to 4 hours of playback with just 10 minutes of USB-C charging. The Studio Pro offers excellent cross-OS compatibility, including quick pairing and Find My capabilities. However, features like spatial audio and dynamic head tracking are exclusive to iPhone owners.

That said, the Studio Pro omits some features that are standard on other premium models, like ear detection, so they don’t automatically pause the music when you remove them from your head. Still, the headphones provide impressive performance wrapped in the brand’s iconic style.

Read More: Best Workout Clothes For Men, Tested by Us

The Powerbeats Pro 2 by Beats are a fresh addition to the workout headphone market and have quickly become our top choice among models with ear hooks. Nearly six years after the original, Beats has introduced major upgrades like active noise cancellation, transparency mode, and heart rate monitoring—a feature typically found in smartwatches. Setting up these headphones was a breeze, with the same seamless iPhone connectivity you’d expect from AirPods.

We prefer the fit and look of the Powerbeats Pro 2 compared to the first generation. The previous model’s ear hooks felt awkward, but the new design is much more comfortable, with five different ear tip sizes for a secure fit. During testing, these headphones felt glued to our ears—even during a windy run—and didn’t flinch after getting drenched in sweat thanks to the IPX4 rating. The softer plastic avoids the slimy feel after intense workouts, allowing them to transition quickly from the gym to the office.

In addition to a comfortable fit, the sound quality has improved significantly, with balanced bass that enhances the listening experience without overpowering it. We appreciated the intuitive controls, with a button on each earbud for volume control—clicking the right to increase and the left to decrease. Pausing and skipping tracks was also easy by tapping the sides of each earbud. Making calls was straightforward, with excellent sound quality, and the battery percentage displayed more precisely on our phone than other headphones, which often rounded to the nearest 10 percent.

Each earbud features a built-in sensor—the same tech found in the Apple Watch—for heart rate monitoring. It’s a nice feature, providing valuable insights into your performance during workouts. However, if you already own an Apple Watch, this feature might feel redundant.

While there’s a lot to like about the Powerbeats Pro 2, the side buttons can be overly sensitive. During runs, we occasionally found ourselves accidentally pausing or skipping tracks while adjusting the fit or changing the volume. If you own an iPhone, you’ll get the most value from these earbuds, benefiting from features like dynamic head tracking, automatic device switching, and Find My integration. However, Android users can still enjoy the earbuds with one-touch pairing, customizable controls, and the Locate My Beats feature available through the Beats app.

The Shokz OpenRun Pro 2 holds the top spot among the best bone conduction headphones and was featured in the 2025 Men’s Health Tech Awards, so it’s no surprise they’re a favorite here.

The classic wraparound design and open-ear style are incredibly comfortable, particularly during longer runs. The lightweight titanium frame rests against your temples rather than in your ear canals, eliminating the discomfort and ear fatigue you might experience with traditional earbuds. The design feels funky at first, but once you get used to it, you’ll appreciate not having anything in your ears.

After testing the OpenRun Pro 2 on both long and short runs, we found the design goes beyond comfort. Perhaps the most significant advantage is the enhanced safety it provides. Unlike traditional earbuds that can block external sounds, these headphones leave your ear canals completely unobstructed, so you stay fully aware of your surroundings. This is crucial for runners who need to hear approaching vehicles and other hazards.

Despite the open-ear design, the OpenRun Pro 2 produces high-quality sound. Shokz’s DualPitch tech helps eliminate unwanted vibrations, so audio comes through clearly—something bone conduction headphones struggle with. Even in noisy environments like busy streets or gyms, we didn’t have to max out the volume to hear our favorite tunes. In addition to its lightweight design, the headphones offer up to 12 hours of battery life and an IP55 rating, so they’ll hold up to your toughest workouts.

The Bose Ultra Open earbuds depart from traditional in-ear designs, with a clip-on style that rests against your ear rather than inside it. This unconventional approach keeps you aware of your surroundings while still delivering premium features and sound quality. For runners or cyclists, they’re a compelling alternative to conventional earbuds and bone conduction headphones.

Setup is straightforward, with quick pairing with both Android and iOS devices, and a Bose Music app that lets you customize EQ settings and update the firmware. While the design takes some getting used to, particularly getting them just right on your ear, the lightweight design is comfortable, with little to no ear fatigue. We worried the clip-on style would slip during quick head movements, but they stayed secure and never jostled loose.

Sound quality is impressive considering the open design, with Bose’s OpenAudio technology directing sound toward your ears. They don’t match the experience of noise-canceling earbuds, but the audio performance strikes an impressive balance for anyone who needs to remain aware of their surroundings. Meanwhile, the dual-microphone array delivers excellent call quality, even in noisy environments.

Battery life is about 9 hours per charge, with the case providing an additional 19.5 hours. IPX4 water resistance protects against sweat and light rain, making these suitable for most outdoor activities. For users who find traditional earbuds uncomfortable, the Bose Ultra Open is a refreshing alternative while delivering the quality we’ve come to expect from the brand.

At $30, the JLab Go Sport+ is the perfect entry-level option for runners. The flexible earhooks keep the buds locked in place, the sound is decent enough to keep you moving, and an IP55 rating means sweat isn’t a concern. Be Aware mode lets ambient sound in when you need it, and the JLab app offers EQ presets if you want to tweak the sound.

Battery life is another standout, reaching up to 9 hours on a single charge and extending to 35 hours with a case that conveniently includes a built-in USB-C cable. If you just need something reliable that won’t sting to replace when they inevitably go missing, these are it.

The Pixel Buds Pro 2 are the earbuds to get if you own a Pixel phone, offering an integration experience that rivals the seamless connection between AirPods and iPhones. Google’s second-generation earbuds build on their predecessor with improved ANC, a more comfortable design, and enhanced sound quality, making them an easy choice for Google users.

The silicone ear tips create a secure seal, while a small “stabilizer” (basically a tiny fin) on each earbud helps them stay in place when you’re moving around. With an IPX4 rating, the Buds Pro 2 handle sweat without issue, so you can use them on a rainy-day run. The matte finish on both the buds and the case resists fingerprints and maintains a premium feel even after extended use.

Sound quality has been upgraded thanks to new 11mm dynamic drivers. Google’s spatial audio delivers an immersive listening experience for compatible content, while the Tensor-powered adaptive EQ automatically adjusts to your environment. The improved noise cancellation is particularly impressive, effectively blocking noise. Touch controls on each earbud are responsive without being overly sensitive, and the conversation detection feature automatically pauses music when you start speaking.

Battery life is around eight hours with ANC enabled, with the case providing an additional 30 hours. A quick five-minute charge delivers about one-and-a-half hours of listening time in a pinch. Overall, the Buds Pro 2 are the best wireless earbuds for Pixel phone owners, offering excellent comfort, sound quality, and durability. Plus, you get powerful features like Live Translate and Gemini support, making them more than just workout headphones.

Android users have no shortage of options, including those on this list. But Samsung’s extremely lightweight Galaxy Buds 3 Pro stand out, offering excellent features and performance for everyday use and workouts. Their AirPods-like stem design is more comfortable and secure than the Buds 2 Pro, with silicone ear tips (available in 3 sizes) that create an effective seal without causing discomfort during extended wear. They’ll stay firmly in place through running, HIIT workouts, and strength training sessions.

The IP57 rating provides excellent protection against both dust and water, making these earbuds capable of surviving temporary submersion up to 1 meter. If you like to rough it while working out, these earbuds are durable enough. The Galaxy Buds 3 Pro earbuds also deliver high-quality sound, with an adaptive mode that blends ANC based on your surroundings. These features work seamlessly, so you can hear important sound cues like sirens more easily.

Powerful voice controls elevate the hands-free experience, letting you manage playback, adjust volume, and toggle between listening modes without a specific wake phrase—simply say “volume up,” and the earbuds will increase the volume. While these earbuds get just about everything right, they lack true multipoint connectivity, meaning they can’t maintain simultaneous active connections to multiple devices. However, Samsung users benefit from Auto Switch technology, which automatically transitions the earbuds between your Galaxy phone, tablet, and laptop whenever you start media on a different device.

For people whose ears tend to reject standard earbuds, the Powerbeats Fit offer a secure alternative with its flexible wingtips and snug, workout-friendly design. While the unusual fit takes some getting used to, our testing found the buds stayed put during HIIT, running, strength training, and everyday use.

"They're still the finest, and steadiest fitness earbuds out there," says Fitness Director Ebenezer Samuel, C.S.C.S. "The latest iteration doesn't change too much of the (very effective) formula they've had, which is just fine because they work so well."

Fit can vary by ear shape, and one of our testers initially experienced soreness from the wingtips—an issue that eased with shorter wear times and a switch to smaller ear tips. Once adjusted, the seal improved both comfort and sound quality. The wingtips effectively prevent slippage during intense workouts, though you need to place them carefully to keep them secure.

Sound quality is strong for workout earbuds, with active noise cancellation and transparency modes that are easy to toggle in the app, though not quite as robust as the AirPods Pro. Controls are simple, with identical tap functions on each side, plus handy features like auto-pause and magnetic charging-case guidance. The 30-hour battery life is a major advantage.

We were a little bummed that the box doesn't include a USB-C charging cable or on-earbud volume control. While other headphones with ear hooks may feel even more secure, the Powerbeats Fit offers a compelling blend of stability, sound, and battery life. At $200, they’re a worthwhile pick for anyone who needs earbuds that reliably stay put during tough workouts.

Read More: Our Full Review of the Powerbeats Fit

Open earbuds let outside noise in by design, but that same openness can make it hard to hear your audio on a busy street. The Shokz OpenFit Pro tackles this with a triple-microphone array and adaptive algorithm that dampens outside noise without sealing your ear canal. It won’t replace full ANC, but it quiets things just enough that you can actually enjoy what you’re listening to. Sound quality has also improved over previous OpenFit models, with better bass response and zero distortion at higher volumes.

The soft silicone ear hooks sit behind your ears, keeping the buds stable during runs and listening sessions—and because of the open-ear design, you stay aware of your surroundings the entire time—a real safety advantage over traditional earbuds. Battery life is solid, too, stretching 12 hours without noise reduction and extending to 50 hours total with the case.

JBL has always known how to do bass, and the Endurance Race 2 lives up to that reputation. The 6.8mm dynamic drivers deliver a low-end that’s full and punchy without overpowering the mids, which is exactly what you want when a heavy playlist is the only thing getting you through a final set. JBL’s Twistlock wing design twists into your ear, keeping them in place during jumping exercises.

The IP68 rating means these can handle full submersion, not just sweat. ANC and Smart Ambient mode let you toggle between blocking out the gym and staying aware of traffic on a run. Battery life tops out at 12 hours per charge, with the case extending that to 48 hours total. That’s enough to last a long weekend without needing to find a wall outlet.

If you go hard during your workouts, you'll sweat—probably a lot. You need headphones that can keep up. Look for headphones or earbuds that are at least water-resistant or, even better, waterproof. Really, earbuds usually offer better sweat resistance than over-ear headphones. Over-ear headphones with sweat-resistant construction are a lot harder to find, although brands like Sony and Beats still hold up well to sweat. If most of your workouts are moderate, sweat resistance may not matter, and any pair of the workout headphones we've noted below will work for you.

Your favorite gym clothes aren't the only things drenched in sweat after a workout. So durability for workout headphones starts with water and sweat protection, measured by the standardized Ingress Protection (IP) rating system. The rating has two numbers: the first indicates dust protection (6 being the highest), and the second indicates water resistance (9 being the highest).

For serious athletes (and seriously sweaty workouts), we recommend headphones that offer water protection to survive intense training sessions. If you primarily work out indoors, you can get by without dust protection, but consider proper dust resistance if you’re trail running or doing HIIT on the beach. Beyond dust and water resistance, the best workout headphones boast impact-resistant materials, such as durable plastic, high-grade silicone or rubber, and reinforced hinges and headbands.

Comfort and fit are essential for peak performance. While these factors can be highly personal, certain features separate the headphones you’ll love wearing from those that’ll end up at the bottom of your gym bag.

Several types of workout headphones suit different preferences. In-ear models are the most popular for workouts thanks to their compact size, and they come in several variations. The first type features interchangeable silicone tips in small, medium, and large sizes, creating a customized seal in your ear canal. Another design incorporates flexible ear hooks that loop over your ears for added stability.

If you don’t like wearing earbuds, on-ear headphones provide cushioned pads that rest against your ears, while over-ear models create a complete seal around each ear. Bone conduction headphones offer a unique solution by conducting sound through your cheekbones while keeping your ear canals open. These are a good option for anyone who needs to maintain awareness of their surroundings, such as when running at night or in a busy city.

When you’re crushing a grueling rowing routine or leg day, the last thing you want is your headphones dying mid-workout. Look for wireless earbuds that provide at least six to eight hours of continuous playback, or on-ear/over-ear headphones that can go 20+ hours on a single charge. That should last through a full week of workouts.

The good news is most earbuds come with charging cases that can extend total battery life by several hours; it’s like having a portable battery with you at all times. Additionally, features like wireless charging and fast charging make it even more convenient to keep your headphones powered up, whether you’re dropping them on a charging pad before bed or need a quick boost before heading out the door.

Obtuse controls can throw off your entire rhythm, so look for earbuds and headphones with simple buttons or touch controls. The best workout headphones feature intuitive controls that let you skip tracks, adjust the volume, and answer phone calls—all without touching your phone. Some headphones even offer customizable controls through companion apps, allowing you to tailor the experience to your needs.

Connectivity should be equally seamless. That means pairing quickly with your smartphone without jumping through menus and Bluetooth settings. For instance, the AirPods Pro 3 and Beats Powerbeats Pro 2 are easy to pair with an iPhone—simply open the earbuds’ case near your device and tap to connect. Once paired, your AirPods can automatically connect to all your Apple devices through iCloud. Android users can take advantage of Fast Pair technology, which offers a similar one-tap connection experience with compatible headphones.

Additionally, many models now support multipoint pairing, allowing you to seamlessly switch between your phone and computer without manually disconnecting and reconnecting your headphones each time. Voice assistant integration adds another layer of hands-free convenience, letting you control your music, set a timer, or make a phone call using just your voice.

For those of us who work in busy offices, active noise cancellation (ANC) can be a game-changer for blocking out distractions. In gyms, this technology is just as useful, drowning out clanking weights, loud conversations, and blaring music.

However, the best workout headphones don’t just block out noise—they also let noise in. For outdoor workouts (or simply walking down the street), a transparency mode is an essential safety feature that uses the headphones’ microphones to pipe in ambient noise, such as approaching vehicles and emergency sirens. The most effective models let you switch between ANC and transparency modes with a simple tap or voice command.

Beyond noise cancellation, adaptive audio can automatically adjust the sound based on your environment, so you don’t have to constantly fiddle with volume controls.

For the past six years, Men's Health's fitness and tech editors have been testing dozens of the top workout headphones for men. Experts, including Fitness Director Ebenezer Samuel, C.S.C.S., Senior Fitness Editor Brett Williams, NASM, Fitness and Commerce Editor Charles Thorp, NASM, and Senior Gear Editor Ryan Brower, have put countless options through the paces in multiple workout types to evaluate their fit, durability, comfort, sound quality, and price throughout all of it.

The testing environments included, but weren't limited to, busy CrossFit boxes, elite performance facilities, mountain trails, garage gyms, and outdoor tracks. We even wrote this story while testing various models to listen to podcasts, tunes, and even books in a busy work environment.

The guide below highlights what we believe are the best workout headphones to know about right now.

Sweat causes a lot of wear and tear on over-ear headphones, especially the ear pads. We recommend using only over-ear headphones for moderate activity, such as lifting and light cardio. You don't really want to wear over-ear headphones for HIIT or other high-cardio workouts, because the more sweat you create, the more likely your headphones are to slip and slide around on your head.

Over-ear headphones do provide great sound quality, and if you are strategic about how you work out with them, you likely won't see sweat as an issue until much farther down the road. In one specific case, one of our editors was a big fan of his Beats Studio Pro headphones for weight lifting. It took about 3 years for his ear pads to wear down from sweat and regular use, so he then bought replacement ear pads for those headphones.

You won't have any problems with sweat and water resistance on the running headphones we curated above; however, some are rated higher than others—which means they're more durable.

When you see an "IP" rating, what you are really reading is the Ingress Protection rating. You can read a rating from each number: the first indicates dust protection, and the second indicates water protection. Some of the headphones above have an "X" as the first number. The X indicates that no data were collected on dust protection. Sometimes it helps to know the dust protection, especially if you're running on trails. If you're a trail runner, it's probably worth paying attention to the more rugged running headphones that also have a dust rating.

The second number indicates how waterproof the headphones are. Zero is the lowest, while 9K is the highest (you're unlikely to see a 9K rating on headphones because this level is reserved for materials that can handle direct, high-temp, high-pressure water).

So what's a good number range to look for? We recommend anything between four and six, but seven and higher is a premium rating and can withstand full submersion in water. Most of what you will see is "IPX4", which is solid, but if you're really wanting a pair of running headphones that are made to handle high activity, then look for a second number of seven or above.

#5045. NYDFS sets fresh advertising standards for crypto firms

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The NYDFS issued the guidance, outlining its expectations for "accurate content, clear disclosures, and an established governance and review process."

New York’s top financial regulator issued fresh guidance outlining expectations for cryptocurrency firms to establish policies and procedures governing advertising and marketing, including measures to prevent false or misleading content, by next year.

On Thursday, the New York State Department of Financial Services issued the guidance, outlining its expectations for "accurate content, clear disclosures, and an established governance and review process," with firms expected to implement the measures by May 10, 2027.

"For more than a decade, DFS has set the global standard for virtual currency oversight and consumer protection," said Department of Financial Services Acting Superintendent Kaitlin Asrow in the statement. "Today’s guidance reinforces our commitment to protecting New Yorkers and ensures advertising and marketing practices in the virtual currency space are clear, comprehensive, and accessible."

NYDFS has long been a leading regulator of the crypto industry, launching its landmark BitLicense regime in 2015 and helping shape the broader regulatory approach to digital assets in the U.S. Other regulators globally have also turned their attention to advertisements, including in the UK.

The NY regulator said virtual currency entities' advertising should not be deceptive, should not include any exaggerated claims or say that something has no fee trading while omitting other costs. The entities also need to file disclosures on if people making testimonials were compensated, the regulator said.

Entities also need to have a governance and review structure that includes clear roles and responsibilities for advertising and marketing, the regulator added.

#5044. EU DeFi proposals could push users to riskier platforms, says Coinbase exec

Coinbase’s EU policy chief says applying suitability tests beyond crypto lending and borrowing could limit access and fragment liquidity.

European Union efforts to tighten oversight of decentralized finance (DeFi) could drive crypto users toward platforms offering fewer investor protections, according to Coinbase’s head of EU policy.

Roeland Van der Stappen told Cointelegraph that regulated crypto platforms could respond by limiting or dropping access to DeFi services.

“Rules designed to protect investors would end up pushing activity towards the places with the least protection,” Van der Stappen said.

In its September response to the European Commission’s consultation on the Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA), the European Securities and Markets Authority recommended creating a new category of regulated crypto services for firms that give customers access to DeFi protocols.

In September, the European Banking Authority (EBA) urged the Commission to study new rules for crypto lending and borrowing, including when regulated platforms give customers access to DeFi lending protocols. It listed suitability tests, which assess whether a service is appropriate for a customer, as one option in its consultation response.

Related: France proposes liability for crypto exchanges offering DeFi access

Van der Stappen said that extending such tests to other DeFi services could mean treating a simple stablecoin swap the same way as leveraged lending, despite their different risks.

Lending, liquidity provision and on-chain swaps would be the most exposed activities, he said. Some providers may narrow their offerings or even withdraw DeFi access altogether.

“A proportionate approach would be to allow CASPs [crypto-asset service providers] to continue offering that access with safeguards matched to the activity,” Van der Stappen said.

Van der Stappen said restricting access through regulated platforms could split DeFi liquidity into smaller pools and leave EU users with worse prices on trades.

He said the restrictions could encourage “walled gardens,” or isolated DeFi services, and steer users toward providers outside the EU with fewer safeguards.

A 2026 Adan-Ipsos survey found that 49% of French crypto buyers preferred accessing DeFi through exchanges, compared with 14% who favored personal wallets.

A Chainalysis report on Monday attributed a modest decline in crypto activity in Germany largely to declining decentralized exchange activity. Across the EU, crypto activity grew 2.2% in the 12 months through June 2026.

A 2025 Chainalysis report described DeFi as a small but growing segment of Europe’s crypto market. In the European Economic Area, May 2025 was the strongest month for DeFi lending activity in the period Chainalysis examined.

Magazine: Stablecoins can drain from banks and nations at lightning speed
